Time periodic problem of compressible Euler equations with damping on the whole space
Houzhi Tang, Kazuyuki Tsuda

Abstract
In this article, time periodic problem of the compressible Euler equations with damping on the whole space is studied. It is well known that in the Euler system, long-time behavior of solutions is a more delicate problem due to lack of the viscosity. By virtue of a damping effect, time global solutions barely exist. Under such circumstances, existence of a time periodic solution is obtained for sufficiently small time periodic external force when the space dimension is greater than or equal to 3 3 . In addition, its stability is also obtained. The solution is asymptotically stable under sufficiently small initial perturbations and the L ∞ L^{\infty} norm of the perturbation decays as time goes to infinity. The potential theoretical estimates work well on a low frequency part of solutions, while a new energy estimate with weights is established to avoid derivative loss.



2020 MR Subject Classification:
35Q31, 35B10, 35B35, 35B40.

Key words:
Euler equations with damping, time periodic problem, spectral analysis

1
Introduction

We consider time periodic problem of the following Euler equations with damping in
\mathbb{R}^{d}

(d\geq 3)
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}\rho+\mbox{\rm div}\,(\rho v)=0,\\
&\partial_{t}(\rho v)+\mbox{\rm div}\,(\rho v\otimes v)+\nabla P(\rho)+\rho v=\rho g,\end{aligned}\right.

(1.1)

where
\rho=\rho(x,t)
denotes the unknown density, and

v={}^{\top}{(v_{1}(x,t),\cdots,v_{d}(x,t))}
is the corresponding velocity field, respectively, at time
t\geq 0
and
position
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
;
The pressure
P=P(\rho)
is supposed to be a smooth function of
\rho
satisfying

P^{\prime}(\rho_{*})>0

for a given positive constant
\rho_{*}
; The function
g=g(x,t)
is given external force satisfying the condition

\begin{array}[]{rcl}g(x,t+T)&=&g(x,t)\quad(x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\,t\in\mathbb{R})\end{array}

(1.2)

for some constant
T>0
.

Time periodic flow is one of basic phenomena in fluid mechanics, and thus, time periodic problems for
fluid dynamical equations have been extensively studied. We refer, e.g., to
[
12
,
15
,
20
,
30
]
for the incompressible Navier-Stokes case, and to
[
2
,
4
,
5
,
10
,
27
,
28
]

for the compressible case.
In this paper we are interested in time periodic problem for the compressible fluid system without the viscosity terms on unbounded domains.
As well known, solutions to the compressible fluid system without the viscosity terms, i.e., the compressible Euler system blow up. Indeed, classical results, John
[
7
]
, Liu
[
14
]
and Li, Zhou and Kong
[
17
]
confirmed that when initial data are small smooth perturbations near constant states, blowup in gradient of solutions occurs in finite time. We note that on the incompressible case, the blow up problem has been a big problem in long time, and recently Eligindi
[
3
]
obtained an important progress, i.e., some blow up results of solutions.

Therefore, as one of continuing works, adding some damping terms to the system has been considered to get time global solutions. We focus on the compressible Euler system with damping (
1.1
) as a typical system.
Recently, study of the compressible Euler system with damping has been developed by many researchers. In multi space dimensions, Wang and Yang
[
29
]
first studied time global solutions with time asymptotic behavior of them to (
1.1
). Then
Sideris, Thomases, and Wang
[
24
]
obtained the global smooth solutions under small data assumption, and proved the singularity formation of solutions for a class of large data. On the initial value problem, here we refer to Ji and Mei
[
6
]
. They investigated the influence of the damping effect to time-decay estimates of solutions and showed that if the damping effect depends time and becomes weak as
t\rightarrow\infty
, the solutions may have time growth order estimates.
On the other hand, concerning the time periodic problem, as far as we investigated, there are few results. Tan, Xu and Wang
[
25
]
studied in a periodic domain. They showed that under some smallness and symmetry assumptions on the external force, we have the existence of the periodic solutions. Li and Yao
[
16
]
proved the existence of time periodic solutions to the full quantum Euler equation on
\mathbb{R}^{3}
for a small time periodic external force.

Let us explain why we consider the time periodic problem and difficulty of the problem. The problem is deeply related to time asymptotic behavior of solutions.
The difficulty is that we consider the unbounded domains which gives polynomial time-decay rate of solutions, with lower regularity solution spaces by lack of the viscosity.

On the compressible Navier-Stokes system,
Ma, Ukai, and Yang
[
27
]
proved the existence and stability of time periodic solutions on the whole space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
d\geq 5
.
Since time periodic solution is one of time global solutions, we have to use the information of time asymptotic behavior of linear system. Hence they consider the higher dimension case
d\geq 5
because
the solution has faster decay rates as the hear kernel. We note that
if we consider bounded domain case like a periodic domain, linear solutions have exponential decay rates in each dimension.
It was shown in
[
10
]
that,
for
d\geq 3
, if the external force
g
satisfies the same symmetry condition
as
[
25
]
, there exists a time periodic solution.
The symmetry condition gives faster decay rate of linear solutions.
After that, Tsuda
[
26
]
proved the existence of the time periodic solution of the compressible Navier-Stokes equations without symmetric condition for the external force by applying the potential theoretical method.

For (
1.1
), the long-time behavior is more delicate by lack of the viscosity as above previous works. Indeed,
if there is no damping, solutions to the Euler system blow up in finite time, which can be referred to
[
23
]
. If the damping effect becomes weaker as
t\rightarrow\infty
,
the solutions also may blow up in infinite time.
By virtue of a firm damping effect,
we barely have time global solutions. This motivate us to consider time periodic problem related to time asymptotic behavior of solutions.

Furthermore, the lack of viscosity causes another serious problem: we have to consider lower order regularity solution space in the energy estimates.
Since we consider the hyperbolic system, we have to take care of derivative loss of the density part of solutions like the compressible Navier-Stokes system. This technique is called as the Matsumura-Nishida energy estimate. In addition, for the Euler-damping system, derivative loss of the velocity part also has to be taken care of because there is no dispersion term about the velocity. We note that if we consider the full quantum Euler equation, we can use the dispersion term in the energy estimates and the density has higher order regularity.

We consider (
1.1
). It will be proved that if
d\geq 3
and if
g
satisfies (
1.2
) and

\displaystyle\|g\|_{C([0,T];L^{1})}+\|(1+|x|^{d})g\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty})}+\|(1+|x|^{d-1})g\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s-1})}\ll 1

with an integer
s\geq[d/2]+2
, then
there exists a time periodic solution

(\rho_{per},v_{per})\in C([0,T];H^{s})
with period
T
for (
1.1
)
and
u_{per}(t)=(\rho_{per}(t)-\rho_{\ast},v_{per}(t))
satisfies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\sum_{j=0}^{1}\|(1+|x|^{d-2+j})\partial_{x}^{j}\rho_{per}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|(1+|x|^{d-1})v_{per}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}})

\displaystyle\quad\leq C(\|g\|_{C([0,T];L^{1})}+\|(1+|x|^{d})g\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty})}+\|(1+|x|^{d-1})g\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s-1})}).

(1.3)

Furthermore, if
g
satisfies

\|g\|_{C([0,T];L^{1})}+\|(1+|x|^{d})g\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty})}+\|(1+|x|^{d-1})g\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s})}\ll 1,

then the time periodic solution
(\rho_{per},v_{per})
is asymptotically stable
under sufficiently small initial perturbations,
and the perturbation satisfies

\|(\rho(t),v(t))-(\rho_{per}(t),v_{per}(t))\|_{L^{\infty}}\rightarrow 0

as
t\rightarrow\infty
.
The precise statements of our existence and stability results
are given in Theorem
3.1
and Theorem
3.3
below.

Let us explain our method to prove the main results.
The existence of a time periodic solution around
(\rho_{*},0)
is shown by an iteration argument
using the time-
T
-map associated with the linearized problem at
(\rho_{*},0)
.
As in
[
10
,
26
]
we formulate the time periodic problem
as a system of equations for low frequency part and high frequency part of the solution. We need to investigate

(I-S_{j}(T))^{-1}
for
j=1
implying low frequency part and
j=\infty
implying the high frequency part respectively. Here
S_{j}(T)=e^{-TA}
with
A
being the linearized operator around
(\rho_{\ast},0)
which
acts on functions whose Fourier transforms have their supports in
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d};|\xi|\leq r_{1}\}
for some
r_{1}>0
or
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d};|\xi|\geq r_{\infty}\}
for some
r_{\infty}>0
. Concerning the low frequency part, we apply the potential theoritical estimates, while the weighted energy estimates is used in the high frequency part as in
[
26
]
.

There are two key observations. One is the decay of solution
v
as
|x|\rightarrow\infty
is faster than

[
26
]
by virtue of the hyperbolic system. In
[
26
]
,
since the spatial decay at infinity coincides with that of the fundamental solution of the diffusion equation by the viscosity, we need more delicate analysis to estimate the convective term
v\cdot\nabla v
, i.e., to reformulate to the momentum equation to get the divergence form. However, in our system
v
is governed by the hyperbolic system due to lack of the viscosity, which derives faster spatial decay at infinity than
[
26
]
. Hence we can omit such a detailed analysis. We note that on the density part, the solution has slow spatial decay at infinity, however, the nonlinear term related to the density, that is, the pressure term
\nabla(P(\rho)\rho)
has already the derivative form like the divergence form. This implies possibility that time periodic solution can be constructed easier than
the usual compressible Navier-Stokes system although there are few previous results on the Euler system with damping terms so far.

Another point is techniques to avoid derivative loss in the energy estimates.
As for the high frequency part, we employ the weighted energy estimates
established in
[
10
]
. Since the solution belongs to
C([0,T];H^{s})
, especially, there is no dispersion term on
v
due to Euler system, we have to take care of derivative loss in the energy estimate. In taking care of the derivative loss, we cannot apply the previous method used in the initial value problem as in
[
29
]
. Because when we use the iteration, the numbers of solutions between linear part and nonlinear part is different, i.e., there appears, for example,

(v^{(k-1)}\cdot\nabla v^{(k-1)},v^{(k)})_{L^{2}}

in the energy estimate, where
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence the integration by parts does not work for such a term to avoid derivative loss. To overcome this difficulty, we extend the Matsumura-Nishida energy estimate. We regard such nonlinear terms as linear terms by taking one of solutions as given functions, for instance,
\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v
with a given function
\tilde{v}
. Then we include the linear terms to the linear system. See (
6.17
) below. We establish weighted energy estimates for (
6.17
). We note that in the weighted energy estimates, some reminder terms related to weights appear, for example,

(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v,\nabla(|x|^{\ell})v)_{L^{2}},

where
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
However, such terms do not have any derivative loss of solutions because derivatives act to the weights. This implies that weighted energy estimates can be applicable to the Euler system.

Finally, the asymptotic stability of the time periodic solution
(\rho_{per},v_{per})
, which had not been obtained in previous results, can be proved as in
the argument in Kagei and Kawashima
[
8
]
by using the Hardy inequality.

This paper is organized as follows.
In section
2
, we introduce notations and auxiliary lemmas used in this paper.
In section
3
, we state main results of this paper.
Section
4
is devoted to the reformulation of the problem.
We will rewrite the system for the low and high frequency parts into a system of integral equations in terms of the time-
T
-map.
In section
5
, we study the low frequency part and derive the necessary estimates
for the time-
T
-map of the low frequency part.
In section
6
, we state some spectral properties of
the time-
T
-map of the high frequency part.
In section
7
, we estimate nonlinear terms
and then give a proof of the existence of a time periodic solution by the iteration argument. In section
8
, we prove the stability of the time periodic solution.

2
Preliminaries

In this section we first introduce some notations which will be used throughout this paper. We then introduce some auxiliary lemmas which will be useful in the proof of the main results.

For a given Banach space
X,
the norm on
X
is denoted by
\|\cdot\|_{X}
.

Let
1\leqq p\leqq\infty.
We denote by
L^{p}
the usual
L^{p}
space over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
The inner product of
L^{2}
is denoted by
(\cdot,\cdot)
.
For a nonnegative integer
k
, we denote by
H^{k}
the usual
L^{2}
-Sobolev space of order
k
.
(As usual,
H^{0}=L^{2}
.)

We simply denote by
L^{p}
the set of all vector fields

v={}^{\top}(v_{1},\cdots,v_{d})
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}

with
v_{j}\in L^{p}

(j=1,\cdots,d)
, i.e.,
(L^{p})^{d}

and the norm
\|\cdot\|_{(L^{p})^{d}}
on it is denoted by
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}}

if no confusion will occur.
Similarly, for a function space
X
,
the set of all vector fields

v={}^{\top}(v_{1},\cdots,v_{d})
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}

with
v_{j}\in X

(j=1,\cdots,d)
, i.e.,
X^{d}
, is simply denoted by
X
;
and the norm
\|\cdot\|_{X^{d}}
on it is denoted by
\|\cdot\|_{X}
if no confusion will occur.
(For example,
(H^{k})^{d}
is simply denoted by
H^{k}

and the norm
\|\cdot\|_{(H^{k})^{d}}
is denoted by
\|\cdot\|_{H^{k}}
.)

Let
u={}^{\top}(a,v)
with
\phi\in H^{k}
and
v={}^{\top}(v_{1},\cdots,v_{d})\in H^{m}
.
we denote the norm of
u
on
H^{k}\times H^{m}
by
\|u\|_{H^{k}\times H^{m}}
:

\|u\|_{H^{k}\times H^{m}}=\left(\|a\|_{H^{k}}^{2}+\|v\|_{H^{m}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

When
m=k
, the space
H^{k}\times(H^{k})^{d}
is simply denoted by
H^{k}
, and, also,
the norm
\|u\|_{H^{k}\times(H^{k})^{d}}
by
\|u\|_{H^{k}}

if no confusion will occur :

H^{k}:=H^{k}\times(H^{k})^{d},\ \ \ \|u\|_{H^{k}}:=\|u\|_{H^{k}\times(H^{k})^{d}}\ \ \ (u={}^{\top}(a,v)).

Similarly, for
u={}^{\top}(a,v)\in X\times Y
with
v={}^{\top}(v_{1},\cdots,v_{d})

,
we denote its norm
\|u\|_{X\times Y}
by
\|u\|_{X\times Y}
:

\|u\|_{X\times Y}=\left(\|a\|_{X}^{2}+\|v\|_{Y}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\ \ \ (u={}^{\top}(a,v)).

If
Y=X^{d}
,
we simply denote
X\times X^{d}
by
X
,
and, its norm
\|u\|_{X\times X^{d}}
by
\|u\|_{X}
:

X:=X\times X^{d},\ \ \ \|u\|_{X}:=\|u\|_{X\times X^{d}}\ \ \ (u={}^{\top}(a,v)).

We will work on function spaces with spatial weight.
For a nonnegative integer
\ell
and
1\leq p\leq\infty
, we denote by
L^{p}_{\ell}
the weighted
L^{p}
space defined by

L^{p}_{\ell}=\{u\in L^{p};\|u\|_{L^{p}_{\ell}}:=\|(1+|x|)^{\ell}u\|_{L^{p}}<\infty\}.

We denote the Fourier transform of
f
by
\hat{f}
or
\mathcal{F}[f]
:

\displaystyle\hat{f}(\xi)=\mathcal{F}[f](\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(x)e^{-ix\cdot\xi}dx\quad(\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}).

The inverse Fourier transform of
f
is denoted by
\mathcal{F}^{-1}[f]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}[f](x)=(2\pi)^{-d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi\quad(x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer and let

r_{1}
and
r_{\infty}
be positive constants satisfying
r_{1}<r_{\infty}.
We denote by
H_{(\infty)}^{k}
the set of all
u\in H^{k}
satisfying
\mbox{supp }\hat{u}\subset\{|\xi|\geq r_{1}\}
,
and by
L_{(1)}^{2}
the set of all
u\in L^{2}
satisfying
\mbox{supp }\hat{f}\subset\{|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}\}
.
Note that
H^{k}\cap L_{(1)}^{2}=L_{(1)}^{2}
for any nonnegative integer
k
. (Cf., Lemma
4.1
(ii) bellow.)

Let
k
and
\ell
be nonnegative integers. We define the spaces
H_{\ell}^{k}
and
H_{(\infty),\ell}^{k}
by

\displaystyle H_{\ell}^{k}=\{u\in H^{k};\|u\|_{H_{\ell}^{k}}<+\infty\},

where

\displaystyle\|u\|_{H_{\ell}^{k}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}|u|_{H^{k}_{j}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle|u|_{H^{k}_{\ell}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k}\parallel|x|^{\ell}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}u\parallel_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

and

\displaystyle H_{(\infty),\ell}^{k}=\{u\in H_{(\infty)}^{k};\|u\|_{H_{\ell}^{k}}<+\infty\}.

Let
\ell
be a nonnegative integer. We denote
L^{2}_{(1),\ell}
by

L^{2}_{(1),\ell}=\{f\in L^{2}_{\ell};f\in L^{2}_{(1)}\}.

For
-\infty\leq a<b\leq\infty
,
we denote by
C^{k}([a,b];X)
the set of all
C^{k}
functions on
[a,b]
with values in
X
.
We denote the Bochner space on
(a,b)
by
L^{p}(a,b;X)

and the
L^{2}
-Bochner-Sobolev space of order
k
by
H^{k}(a,b;X)
.

The space
{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}
is defined by

\displaystyle{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}=\{a\in L^{\infty}_{d-2},\nabla{a}\in H^{1};\mbox{supp }\hat{a}\subset\{|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}\},\|a\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}}<+\infty\},

where

\displaystyle\|a\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}}:=\|a\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}+\|a\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}},

\displaystyle\|a\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}:=\sum_{j=0}^{1}\|(1+|x|)^{d-2+j}{\nabla}^{j}a\|_{L^{\infty}},\,\|a\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}:=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|(1+|x|)^{j-1}{\nabla}^{j}a\|_{L^{2}}.

We define the space
{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}
by

\displaystyle{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}=\{v\in L^{\infty}_{d-1}\cap L^{2},\nabla v\in L^{2}_{1};\mbox{supp }\hat{v}\subset\{|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}\},\|v\|_{{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}}<+\infty\},

where

\displaystyle\|v\|_{{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}}:=\|v\|_{{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}+\|v\|_{{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}},

\displaystyle\|v\|_{{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}:=\|(1+|x|)^{d-1}v\|_{L^{\infty}},\,\|v\|_{{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}:=\|v\|_{L^{2}}+\|(1+|x|)\nabla v\|_{L^{2}}.

The space
{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(a,b)
is defined by

\displaystyle{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(a,b)=C^{1}([a,b];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}).

Let
\ell
be a nonnegative integer and let
s
be a nonnegative integer satisfying
s\geq\left[\frac{d}{2}\right]+2.
For
k=s-1,s
,
the space
{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}}^{k}_{(\infty),\ell}(a,b)
is defined by

\displaystyle{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}}^{k}_{(\infty),\ell}(a,b)

\displaystyle=\bigl[C([a,b];H_{(\infty),\ell}^{k})\cap C^{1}(a,b;H_{(\infty)}^{k-1})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\times C([a,b];H_{(\infty),\ell}^{k}).

Let
s
be a nonnegative integer satisfying
s\geq\left[\frac{d}{2}\right]+2.
and let
k=s-1,s
. The space
X^{k}(a,b)
is defined by

\displaystyle X^{k}(a,b)\quad

\displaystyle=\bigl\{\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\};\,

\displaystyle u_{1}\in{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(a,b),u_{\infty}\in{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}}^{k}_{(\infty),d-1}(a,b),

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\phi_{1}\in C([a,b];L^{2}),\,\partial_{t}\phi_{\infty}\in C([a,b];H^{k-1}),\,u_{j}={}^{\top}{(a_{j},v_{j})}\,(j=1,\infty)\bigr\},

equipped with the norm

\displaystyle\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{k}(a,b)}=

\displaystyle\|u_{1}\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(a,b)}+\|u_{\infty}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}}^{k}_{(\infty),d-1}(a,b)}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{t}\phi_{1}\|_{C([a,b];L^{2})}+\|\partial_{t}\phi_{\infty}\|_{C([a,b];H^{k-1})}.

We also introduce function spaces of
T
-periodic functions in
t
.
We denote by
C_{per}(\mathbb{R};X)
the set of all
T
-periodic continuous functions
with values in
X
equipped with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{C([0,T];X)}
;
and we denote by
L^{2}_{per}(\mathbb{R};X)
the set of all
T
-periodic locally square integrable functions
with values in
X
equipped with the norm
\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}(0,T;X)}
.
Similarly,
H^{1}_{per}(\mathbb{R};X)
and
X_{per}^{k}(\mathbb{R})
, and so on, are defined.

For a bounded linear operator
L
on a Banach space
X
, we denote by
r_{X}(L)
the spectral radius of
L
.

For operators
L_{1}
and
L_{2},

[L_{1},L_{2}]
denotes the commutator of
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
:

\displaystyle[L_{1},L_{2}]f=L_{1}(L_{2}f)-L_{2}(L_{1}f).

We next state some lemmas which will be used in the proof of the main results.

We begin with the well-known Sobolev type inequality.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
d\geq 3
and let
s\geq\left[\frac{d}{2}\right]+1.
Then there holds the inequality

\displaystyle\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\nabla f\|_{H^{s-1}}

for
f\in H^{s}.

We next state some inequalities concerned with composite functions.

Lemma 2.2
.

Assume
d\geq 2
and let
s
be an integer satisfying
s\geq\left[\frac{d}{2}\right]+1
.
Let
s_{j}
and
\mu_{j}
(
j=1,\cdots,\ell
)
satisfy
0\leq|\mu_{j}|\leq s_{j}\leq s+|\mu_{j}|
,

\mu=\mu_{1}+\cdots+\mu_{\ell}
,

s=s_{1}+\cdots+s_{\ell}\leq(\ell-1)s+|\mu|
.
Then there holds

\displaystyle\parallel\partial^{\mu_{1}}_{x}f_{1}\cdots\partial^{\mu_{\ell}}_{x}f_{\ell}\parallel_{L^{2}}\leq C\prod_{1\leq j\leq\ell}\parallel f_{j}\parallel_{H^{s_{j}}}.

See, e.g.,
[
9
]
, for the proof of Lemma
2.2
.

Lemma 2.3
.

[
1
, Lemma 2.1]

Let
f,g\in H^{k}\cap L^{\infty}
with
\nabla^{k}f\in L^{\infty}
. Then it holds that for
k\in\mathbb{N}

\|\nabla^{k}(fg)-f\nabla^{k}g\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(\|\nabla f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{k-1}g\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{k}f\|_{L^{2}}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}}).

3
Main results

In this section, we introduce the main results on the existence and stability of a time-periodic solution for system (
1.1
).

Without loss of generality, we assume
\rho_{*}=1
and
P^{\prime}(\rho_{*})=1
. Denote

\displaystyle\rho=1+\phi,\quad a=\log(1+\phi).

Then system (
1.1
) is reformulated as follows,

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}a+\text{div}v+v\cdot\nabla a=0,\\
&\partial_{t}v+\nabla a+v+g_{3}(\phi)\nabla a+v\cdot\nabla v=g,\end{aligned}\right.

(3.1)

where the function
g_{3}(\phi)
is given by

\displaystyle g_{3}(\phi)=P^{\prime}(1+\phi)-P^{\prime}(1)=P^{\prime}(e^{a})-1.

We see that (
1.1
) is rewritten as the following symmetric hyperbolic system.

\partial_{t}u+Au=-B[u]u+G(g),

(3.2)

where

\displaystyle A=\begin{pmatrix}0&\mathrm{div}\\
\nabla&I\\
\end{pmatrix},

(3.3)

\displaystyle B[\tilde{u}]u=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla a\\
\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v+g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\nabla a\\
\end{pmatrix}\mbox{ for }u={}^{\top}(a,v),\,\tilde{u}={}^{\top}(\tilde{a},\tilde{v}),

(3.4)

and

\displaystyle G(g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0\\
g\\
\end{pmatrix}.

(3.5)

We next introduce operators which decompose a function into
its low and high frequency parts.
Operators
P_{1}
and
P_{\infty}
on
L^{2}
are defined by

\displaystyle P_{j}f=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\hat{\chi}_{j}\mathcal{F}[f]\big)\quad(f\in L^{2},j=1,\infty),

where

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{j}(\xi)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\quad(j=1,\infty),\quad 0\leq\hat{\chi}_{j}\leq 1\quad(j=1,\infty),

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{1}(\xi)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}1\quad(|\xi|\leq r_{1}),\\
0\quad(|\xi|\geq r_{\infty}),\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{\infty}(\xi)=1-\hat{\chi}_{1}(\xi),

\displaystyle 0<r_{1}<r_{\infty}.

We fix
0<r_{1}<r_{\infty}<\frac{1}{2}
in such a way that the estimate
(
5.6
) in Lemma
5.3
below holds for
|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}
.

Our result on the existence of a time periodic solution is stated as follows.

Theorem 3.1
.

Let
d\geq 3
and
s
be an integer satisfying
s\geq\left[\frac{d}{2}\right]+2.
Assume that
g(x,t)
satisfies
(
1.2
)
and
g(x,t)\in C_{per}(\mathbb{R};L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}_{d})\cap L^{2}_{per}(\mathbb{R};H^{s-1}_{d-1})
. Set

\displaystyle[g]_{s}=\|g\|_{C([0,T];L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}_{d})}+\|g\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s-1}_{d-1})}.

Then there exist constants
\delta>0
and
C>0
such that if
[g]_{s}\leq\delta,
then the system
(
3.1
)
has a time-periodic solution
u=u_{1}+u_{\infty}
satisfying
\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\in X^{s}_{per}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

with
\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}\leq C[g]_{s}
.
Furthermore, the uniqueness of time periodic solutions of (
3.2
) holds
in the class

\{u={}^{\top}(a,v);\{P_{1}u,P_{\infty}u\}\in X^{s}_{per}(\mathbb{R}),\,\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}\leq C\delta\}
.

Remark 3.2
.

The time periodic solution obtained in Theorem
3.1

satisfies that

{}^{\top}(\nabla a,v)\in C_{per}(\mathbb{R};H^{s-1}_{d-1}\times H^{s}_{d-1}),\ \ \|\nabla a\|_{C_{per}(\mathbb{R};H^{s-1}_{d-1})}\leq C\|\nabla\phi\|_{C_{per}(\mathbb{R};H^{s-1}_{d-1})}

with
a\in C_{per}(\mathbb{R};L^{\infty})
, where

a=\log(1+\phi)
,
\phi=\phi_{1}+\phi_{\infty}
.

We next consider the stability of the time-periodic solution obtained in Theorem
3.1
.

Let
{}^{\top}(a_{per},v_{per})
be the periodic solution given in Theorem
3.1
.
We denote the perturbation by
{u}={}^{\top}{({\psi},{w})},
where
{\psi}=a-a_{per},{w}=v-v_{per}
.
Substituting
a={\psi}+a_{per}
and
v={w}+v_{per}
into (
3.1
), we see that the perturbation
{u}={}^{\top}{({\psi},{w})}
is governed by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}{\psi}+\mbox{\rm div}\,w+v_{per}\cdot\nabla{\psi}+w\cdot\nabla a_{per}={f^{1}(u)},\\
\partial_{t}{w}+w+\nabla\psi+v_{per}\cdot\nabla{w}+{w}\cdot\nabla v_{per}\\
\quad+g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla\psi+g_{5}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla a_{per}=f^{2}(u),\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle f^{1}(u)

\displaystyle=-w\cdot\nabla\psi,

\displaystyle f^{2}(u)

\displaystyle=-{w}\cdot\nabla{w},

\displaystyle g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)

\displaystyle=P^{\prime}(e^{a_{per}+\psi})-1,

\displaystyle g_{5}(a_{per},\psi)

\displaystyle=P^{\prime}(e^{\psi+a_{per}})-P^{\prime}(e^{a_{per}})

\displaystyle=e^{a_{per}}(e^{\psi}-1)\int_{0}^{1}P^{\prime\prime}(a_{per}+\theta(e^{\psi+a_{per}}))d\theta.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

We consider the initial value problem for (
3
) under the initial condition

\displaystyle u|_{t=0}=u_{0}={}^{\top}{(\psi_{0},w_{0})}.

(3.10)

Our result on the stability of the time-periodic solution is stated as follows.

Theorem 3.3
.

Let
d\geq 3
and
s
be an integer satisfying
s\geq\left[\frac{d}{2}\right]+2.
Assume that
g(x,t)
satisfies
(
1.2
)
and
g(x,t)\in C_{per}(\mathbb{R};L^{1}\cap L^{\infty}_{d})\cap L^{2}_{per}(\mathbb{R};H^{s+1}_{d-1})
.
Let
{}^{\top}(a_{per},v_{per})
be the time-periodic solution obtained in Theorem
3.1
, and let
u_{0}\in H^{s}
.
Then there exist constants
\epsilon_{1}>0
and
\epsilon_{2}>0
such that if

\displaystyle[g]_{s+1}+\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}\leq\delta_{0}\leq 1,

there exists a unique global solution
u={}^{\top}{(\psi,w)}
of (
3
)-(
3.10
) satisfying

\displaystyle u\in C([0,\infty);H^{s}),

(3.11)

\displaystyle\|(\psi,w)(t)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}(\|\nabla\psi(\tau)\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\|w(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}^{2})d\tau\leq C\|u_{0}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\ \ (t\in[0,\infty)),

(3.12)

\displaystyle\|u(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\rightarrow 0\ \ (t\rightarrow\infty).

(3.13)

4
Reformulation of the problem

In this section, we reformulate problem (
3.1
).
Inspired by
[
10
]
, to solve the time periodic problem for (
3.1
), we decompose
u
into a low frequency part
u_{1}
and
a high frequency part
u_{\infty}
, and then, we rewrite the problem
into a system of equations for
u_{1}
and
u_{\infty}
.

First of all, we set

\displaystyle u_{1}=P_{1}u,\quad u_{\infty}=P_{\infty}u.

Applying the operators
P_{1}
and
P_{\infty}
to (
3.2
), we obtain,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{1}+Au_{1}=F_{1}(u_{1}+u_{\infty},g),

(4.1)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{\infty}+Au_{\infty}+P_{\infty}(B[u_{1}+u_{\infty}]u_{\infty})=F_{\infty}(u_{1}+u_{\infty},g).

(4.2)

Here

\displaystyle F_{1}(u_{1}+u_{\infty},g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{1}[-B[u_{1}+u_{\infty}](u_{1}+u_{\infty})+G(g)],

\displaystyle F_{\infty}(u_{1}+u_{\infty},g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle P_{\infty}[-B[u_{1}+u_{\infty}]u_{1}+G(g)].

More precisely, one has

\displaystyle F_{1}(u,g)=P_{1}[-B[u]u+G(g)],\quad F_{\infty}(u,g)=P_{\infty}[-B[u]u_{1}+G(g)].

(4.3)

Suppose that
(\ref{eq:(4.2.2)})
and
(\ref{eq:(4.2.3)})
are satisfied by some functions
u_{1}
and
u_{\infty}
.
Then by adding
(\ref{eq:(4.2.2)})
to
(\ref{eq:(4.2.3)})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(u_{1}+u_{\infty})+A(u_{1}+u_{\infty})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-P_{\infty}(B[u_{1}+u_{\infty}]u_{\infty})+(P_{1}+P_{\infty})F(u_{1}+u_{\infty},g)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-B[u_{1}+u_{\infty}](u_{1}+u_{\infty})+G(g).

Set
u=u_{1}+u_{\infty}
, then we have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u+Au+B[u]u=G(g).

Consequently, if we show the existence of a pair of functions

\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}
satisfying

(\ref{eq:(4.2.2)})
-
(\ref{eq:(4.2.3)})
,
then we can obtain a solution
u
of (
3.2
).

For later use, we prepare some inequalities for the low frequency part.
We first derive some properties of
P_{1}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

(i)

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer.
Then
P_{1}
is a bounded linear operator from
L^{2}
to
H^{k}
.
In fact, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{k}P_{1}f\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{2}}\qquad(f\in L^{2}).

As a result, for any
2\leq p\leq\infty
,
P_{1}
is bounded from
L^{2}
to
L^{p}
.

(ii)

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer.
Then there hold the estimates

\|\nabla^{k}f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\|f_{1}\|_{L^{2}}\ \ (f\in L^{2}_{(1)}),

where
2\leq p\leq\infty
.

The proofs of estimates (i) and (ii) are given in
[
10
, Lemma 4.3]
.

The following inequality is concerned with the estimates of the weighted
L^{p}
norm for the low frequency part.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\chi
be a function which belongs to the Schwartz space
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then for a nonnegative integer
\ell
and
1\leq p\leq\infty
, there holds

\displaystyle\||x|^{\ell}(\chi\ast f)\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\{\||x|^{\ell}\chi\|_{L^{1}}\|f\|_{L^{p}}+\|\chi\|_{L^{1}}\||x|^{\ell}f\|_{L^{p}}\}\qquad(f\in L^{p}_{\ell}).

Here
C
is a positive constant depending only on
\ell
.

Proof.

Let
\chi
be a function which belongs to the Schwartz space
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then

\displaystyle||x|^{\ell}(\chi\ast f)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|x|^{\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\chi(x-y)f(y)|dy

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x-y|^{\ell}|\chi(x-y)||f(y)|dy+C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|\chi(x-y)||y|^{\ell}|f(y)|dy.

Therefore, the Young inequality gives

\displaystyle\||x|^{\ell}(\chi\ast f)\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\{\||x|^{\ell}\chi\|_{L^{1}}\|f\|_{L^{p}}+\|\chi\|_{L^{1}}\||x|^{\ell}f\|_{L^{p}}\}\qquad(f\in L^{p}_{\ell}).

This completes the proof.
\hfill\square

Applying Lemma
4.2
, we have the following inequality for the weighted
L^{p}
norm of the low frequency part.

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
k
and
\ell
be nonnegative integers and let
1\leq p\leq\infty
.
Then there holds the estimate

\||x|^{\ell}\nabla^{k}f_{1}\|_{L^{p}}\leq C\||x|^{\ell}f_{1}\|_{L^{p}}\ \ (f\in L^{2}_{(1)}\cap L^{p}_{\ell}).

Proof.

We define a cut-off function
\chi_{0}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\big(\hat{\chi}_{0}\big)
with
\hat{\chi}_{0}
satisfying

\displaystyle\hat{\chi}_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\ \ 0\leq\hat{\chi}_{0}\leq 1,\ \ \hat{\chi}_{0}=1\ \ \mbox{on}\ \ \{|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}\}\ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \mbox{\rm supp}\,\hat{\chi}_{0}\subset\{|\xi|\leq 2r_{\infty}\}.

(4.4)

We note that

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\chi_{0}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)^{-(d+|\alpha|)}\ \ \mbox{for}\ \ |\alpha|\geq 0.

(4.5)

Since
f_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1)}
, we see that
\nabla^{k}f_{1}=(\nabla^{k}\chi_{0})\ast f_{1}

(k\geq 0)
.
Therefore, by Lemma
4.2
, we obtain the desired estimate.
This completes the proof.
\hfill\square

We look for a time periodic solution
u
for the system
(\ref{eq:(4.2.2)})
and
(\ref{eq:(4.2.3)})
. To solve the time periodic problem, we introduce solution operators for the following linear problems:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}u_{1}+Au_{1}=F_{1},\\
u_{1}|_{t=0}=u_{01},\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}u_{\infty}+Au_{\infty}+P_{\infty}(B[\tilde{u}]u_{\infty})=F_{\infty},\\
u_{\infty}|_{t=0}=u_{0\infty},\end{array}\right.

where
\tilde{u}={}^{\top}{(\tilde{a},\tilde{v})},u_{01},u_{0\infty},F_{1}
and
F_{\infty}
are given functions.

To formulate the time periodic problem,
we denote by
S_{1}(t)
the solution operator for
(\ref{eq:(1)})
with
F_{1}=0
,
and by
{{\mbox{\sy S}\,}}_{1}(t)
the solution operator for
(\ref{eq:(1)})
with
u_{01}=0
.
We also denote by
S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(t)
the solution operator for
(\ref{eq:(2)})
with
F_{\infty}=0

and by
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(t)
the solution operator for
(\ref{eq:(2)})
with
u_{0\infty}=0
.
(The precise definition of these operators will be given later.)

Our main goal is to look for a
\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}
satisfying

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}u_{1}(t)=S_{1}(t)u_{01}+{{\mbox{\sy S}\,}}_{1}(t)[F_{1}(u,g)],\\
u_{\infty}(t)=S_{\infty,u}(t)u_{0\infty}+{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,u}(t)[F_{\infty}(u,g)],\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}u_{01}=(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}{{\mbox{\sy S}\,}}_{1}(T)[F_{1}(u,g)],\\
u_{0\infty}=(I-S_{\infty,u}(T))^{-1}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,u}(T)[F_{\infty}(u,g)],\\
\end{array}\right.

u={}^{\top}(a,v)
is a function given by
u_{1}={}^{\top}(a_{1},v_{1})
and
u_{\infty}={}^{\top}(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})
through the relation

\displaystyle a=a_{1}+a_{\infty},\quad v=v_{1}+v_{\infty}.

Therefore if
(I-S_{1}(T))
and
(I-S_{\infty,u}(T))
are invertible in a suitable sense,
then one obtains
(\ref{eq:(3)})
-
(\ref{eq:(4)})
.
So, to obtain a
T
-time periodic solution of (
4.2
) and (
4.1
), we look for a pair of functions
\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}

satisfying (
4
)-(
4
).
We will investigate the solution operators
S_{1}(t)
and
{{\mbox{\sy S}\,}}_{1}(t)
in section
5
; and we state some properties of
S_{{\infty},{u}}(t)

and
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,u}(t)
in section
6
.

In the remaining of this section we introduce some lemmas which will be used in the proof of Theorem
3.1
.

For the analysis of the low frequency part, we will use the following well-known inequalities.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\alpha
and
\beta
be positive numbers satisfying
d<\alpha+\beta
.
Then there holds the following estimate.

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+|x-y|)^{-\alpha}(1+|y|^{2})^{-\frac{\beta}{2}}dy\leq C\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(1+|x|)^{d-(\alpha+\beta)}\ \ (\max\{\alpha,\beta\}<d),\\
(1+|x|)^{-\min\{\alpha,\beta\}}\log|x|\ \ (\max\{\alpha,\beta\}=d),\\
(1+|x|)^{-\min\{\alpha,\beta\}}\ \ (\max\{\alpha,\beta\}>d)\end{array}\right.

for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

The following lemma is related to the estimates for the integral kernels which will appear in the analysis of the low frequency part.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\ell
be a nonnegative integer and
let
E(x)={{\mbox{\sy F}\,}}^{-1}\hat{\Phi}_{\ell}

(x\in\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
where
\hat{\Phi}_{\ell}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}-\{0\})
is a function satisfying

\displaystyle\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}\hat{\Phi}_{\ell}\in L^{1}\quad(|\alpha|\leq d-3+\ell),

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}\hat{\Phi}_{\ell}|\leq C|\xi|^{-2-|\beta|+\ell}\quad(\xi\neq 0,\,|\beta|\geq 0).

Then the following estimate holds for
x\not=0
.

\displaystyle|E(x)|\leq C|x|^{-(d-2+\ell)}.

Lemma
4.5
easily follows from a direct application of
[
21
, Theorem 2.3]
; and we omit the proof.

We will also use the following lemma for the analysis of the low frequency part.

Lemma 4.6
.

(i)

Let
E(x)

(x\in\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a scalar function satisfying

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E(x)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+|x|)^{|\alpha|+d-2}}\ \ (|\alpha|=0,1,2).

(4.19)

Assume that
f
is a scalar function satisfying
\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{1}}<\infty
.
Then there holds the following estimate for
|\alpha|=0,1
.

\displaystyle|[\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E\ast f](x)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+|x|)^{|\alpha|+d-2}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{1}}.

(ii)

Let
E(x)

(x\in\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a scalar function satisfying
(\ref{Shibata-Tanaka katei})
.
Assume that
f
is a scalar function of the form:
f=\partial_{x_{j}}f_{1}
for some
1\leq j\leq d
satisfying
\|\partial_{x_{j}}f_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d-1}}<\infty
.
Then there holds the following estimate for
|\alpha|=0,1
.

\displaystyle|[\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E\ast f](x)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+|x|)^{|\alpha|+d-2}}(\|\partial_{x_{j}}f_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}+\|f_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d-1}}).

(iii)

Let
E(x)

(x\in\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a scalar function satisfying

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E(x)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+|x|)^{|\alpha|+d-1}}\ \ (|\alpha|=0,1).

Assume that
f
is a scalar function satisfying
\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}<\infty
.
Then there holds the following estimate for
|\alpha|=0,1
.

\displaystyle|[\partial^{\alpha}_{x}E\ast f](x)|\leq\frac{C\log{|x|}}{(1+|x|)^{|\alpha|+d-1}}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}.

Lemma
4.6

\rm(i)
and
\rm(ii)
is given in
[
22
, Lemma 2.5]
for
d=3
and the case
d\geq 4
can be proved similarly;
the assertion
\rm(iii)
can be proved by a direct computation based on Lemma
4.4
; and so
the details are omitted here.

The following inequalities will be used to estimate the low frequency part of nonlinear terms.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\ell
be a nonnegative integer satisfying
\ell\geq d-1
and
E(x)
be a scalar function satisfying that

|E(x)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+|x|)^{\ell}}\ \ \mbox{for}\ \ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Then for
f\in L^{2}_{d-1}
, it holds that

\|E\ast f\|_{L^{\infty}_{d-1}}\leq C\{\|(1+|y|)^{-\ell}\|_{L^{2}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{d-1}}+\|f\|_{L^{2}_{d-1}}\}.

Lemma
4.7
easily follows from direct computations; and we omit the proof.

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\chi_{0}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)^{-(d+|\alpha|)}\ \ \mbox{for}\ \ |\alpha|\geq 0.

(4.20)

As for the high frequency part, we have the following inequalities given in
[
10
, Lemma 4.4]
.

Lemma 4.8
.

(i)

Let
k
be a nonnegative integer.
Then
P_{\infty}
is a bounded linear operator on
H^{k}
.

(ii)

There hold the inequalities

\displaystyle\|P_{\infty}f\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}}\ \ (f\in H^{1}),

\displaystyle\|f_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nabla f_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}\ \ (f_{\infty}\in H^{1}_{(\infty)}).

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then there exists a positive constant
C
depending only on
\ell

such that

\displaystyle\|P_{\infty}f\|_{L^{2}_{\ell}}\leq C\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}_{\ell}}.

Lemma
4.9
follows from the inequalities

\||x|^{k}\nabla f_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\geq\frac{r_{1}^{2}}{2}\||x|^{k}f_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-C\||x|^{k-1}f_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\ \ (k=1,\cdots,\ell)

for
f_{\infty}\in H^{1}_{(\infty),\ell}
which are proved in

[
10
, Lemma 4.7]

by using the Plancherel theorem.

5
Properties of
S_{1}(t)
and
{{\mbox{\sy S}\,}}_{1}(t)

In this section we investigate
S_{1}(t)
and
{{\mbox{\sy S}\,}}_{1}(t)
and establish estimates for a solution
u_{1}
of

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{1}+Au_{1}=F_{1}

(5.1)

satisfying
u_{1}(0)=u_{1}(T)
where
F_{1}={}^{\top}(F_{1}^{1},{F}_{1}^{2})
.

We denote by
A_{1}
the restriction of
A
on
{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

(i)

A_{1}
is a bounded linear operator on
{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}

and
S_{1}(t)=e^{-tA_{1}}
is a uniformly continuous semigroup on
{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}
.
Furthermore,
S_{1}(t)
satisfies

S_{1}(t)u_{1}\in C^{1}([0,T^{\prime}];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}),\ \ \partial_{t}S_{1}(\cdot)u_{1}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];L^{2})

for each
u\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}

and all
T^{\prime}>0
,

\partial_{t}S_{1}(t)u_{1}=-A_{1}S_{1}(t)u_{1}\,(=-AS_{1}(t)u_{1}),\ S_{1}(0)u_{1}=u_{1}\ \ \mbox{for \ $u_{1}\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}$,}

\|\partial_{t}^{k}S_{1}(\cdot)u_{1}\|_{C([0,T^{\prime}];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}})}\leq C\|u_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}},

\|\partial_{t}^{k}S_{1}(\cdot)u_{1}\|_{C([0,T^{\prime}];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}})}\leq C\|u_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}

for
u_{1}\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}
,
k=0,1
,

\|\partial_{t}S_{1}(t)u_{1}\|_{C([0,T^{\prime}];L^{2})}\leq C\|u_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}}.

(ii)

Let the operator
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)
be defined by

{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)F_{1}=\int_{0}^{t}S_{1}(t-\tau)F_{1}(\tau)\,d\tau

for
F_{1}\in C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})
.
Then

{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(\cdot)F_{1}\in C^{1}([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})

for each
F_{1}\in C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})
and

\partial_{t}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)F_{1}+A_{1}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)F_{1}=F_{1}(t),\ {\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(0)F_{1}=0,

\|{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(\cdot)F_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}})}\leq C\|F_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)},L^{p})},

\|\partial_{t}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(\cdot)F_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}})}\leq C\|F_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)},L^{p})},

for
p=2,\infty
,
where
C
is a positive constant depending on
T
.
If, in addition,
F_{1}\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{1})
, then
\partial_{t}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(\cdot)F_{1}\in C([0,T];L^{2})
,

\|\partial_{t}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(\cdot)F_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{2})}\leq C\|F_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{2})},

where
C
is a positive constant depending on
T
.

(iii)

It holds that

S_{1}(t){\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t^{\prime})F_{1}={\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t^{\prime})[S_{1}(t)F_{1}]

for any
t\geq 0
,
t^{\prime}\in[0,T]
and
F_{1}\in C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})
.

Proof of Proposition

5.1
.

Let

\displaystyle\hat{A}_{\xi}=\begin{pmatrix}0&i{}^{\top}{\xi}\\
i\xi&I\end{pmatrix}\ \ \ (\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Then,
{\cal F}(Au_{1})=\hat{A}_{\xi}\hat{u}_{1}
.
Hence, if
{\rm supp}\,\hat{u}_{1}\subset\{\xi;|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}\},
then

{\rm supp}\,\hat{A}_{\xi}\hat{u}_{1}\subset\{\xi;|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}\}.

Furthermore,
we see from Lemma
4.3
that

\|Au_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}}\leq C\|u_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}}

for
p=2,\infty
.
Therefore,
A_{1}
is a bounded linear operator on
{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}
.
It then follows that

-A_{1}
generates a uniformly continuous semigroup
S_{1}(t)=e^{-tA_{1}}

that is given by

S_{1}(t)u_{1}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}e^{-t\hat{A}_{\xi}}\mathcal{F}u_{1}\ \ \ (u_{1}\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}).

Furthermore,
S_{1}(t)
satisfies
S_{1}(\cdot)u_{1}\in C^{1}([0,T^{\prime}];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})

for each
u\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}

, and

\partial_{t}S_{1}(t)u_{1}=-A_{1}S_{1}(t)u_{1}\,(=-AS_{1}(t)u_{1}),\ S_{1}(0)u_{1}=u_{1}\ \ \mbox{for \ $u_{1}\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}$.}

It easily follows from the definition of
S_{1}(t)
that

\|S_{1}(\cdot)u_{1}\|_{C([0,T^{\prime}];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}})}\leq C\|u_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}}\ \ (p=2,\infty)\ \ \mbox{for \ $u_{1}\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}$,}

and hence, by the relation that
\partial_{t}S_{1}(t)u_{1}=-A_{1}S_{1}(t)u_{1}
and Lemma
4.3
,

\|\partial_{t}S_{1}(\cdot)u_{1}\|_{C([0,T^{\prime}];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}})}\leq C\|u_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}}\ \ (p=2,\infty)\ \ \mbox{for \ $u_{1}\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}$, }

where
T^{\prime}>0
is any given positive number and
C
is a positive constant depending on
T^{\prime}
.
In addition, we see from the relation
\partial_{t}S_{1}(t)u_{1}=-A_{1}S_{1}(t)u_{1}
that

\partial_{t}S_{1}(\cdot)u_{1}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];L^{2})
,

\|\partial_{t}S_{1}(\cdot)u_{1}\|_{C([0,T^{\prime}];L^{2})}\leq C\|u_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}}.

The assertion (ii) follows from Lemma
4.3
, the assertion (i) and the relation

\partial_{t}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)[F_{1}]=-A_{1}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)[F_{1}]+F_{1}(t)
.
The assertion (iii) easily follows from the definitions of
S_{1}(t)
and
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)
.
This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

We next investigate invertibility of
I-S_{1}(T)
.

Proposition 5.2
.

If
{F}_{1}
satisfies the conditions given in
{\rm(i)}
-
{\rm(iii)}
, then,
there uniquely exists
u\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}
that satisfies
(I-S_{1}(T))u={F}_{1}
and
u
satisfies
the estimates in each case of
{\rm(i)}
-
{\rm(iii)}
.

(i)

{F}_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1),1}\cap L^{\infty}\cap L^{1}
;

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}\leq C\{\|F_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}+\|F_{1}\|_{L^{1}}\},

(5.2)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}\leq C(\|F_{1}\|_{L^{1}}+\|F_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{1}}).

(5.3)

(ii)

{F}_{1}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{(1),1}
with
F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1)}\cap L^{\infty}_{d-1}

for some
\alpha
satisfying
|\alpha|=1
;

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}\leq C\{\|F_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}+\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d-1}}\},

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}\leq C(\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|F_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{1}}).

(iii)

{F}_{1}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1)}
with
F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1),1}\cap L^{\infty}_{d}

for some
\alpha
satisfying
|\alpha|\geq 1
;

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}},

(5.4)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{1}}.

(5.5)

To prove Proposition
5.2
, we prepare some lemmas.

Lemma 5.3
.

(
[
29
]
)

(i)

The set of all eigenvalues of
-\hat{A}_{\xi}
consists of
\lambda_{j}(\xi)\,(j=0,\pm)
,
where

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}=-1,\quad\lambda_{\pm}(\xi)=\frac{1}{2}(-1\pm\sqrt{1-4|\xi|^{2}}).

We note that

\lambda_{+}=-|\xi|^{2}+O(|\xi|^{4}),\ \ \lambda_{-}=-1+|\xi|^{2}+O(|\xi|^{4})

as
|\xi|\rightarrow 0
.
If
|\xi|>\frac{1}{2}
, then

{\rm Re}\,\lambda_{\pm}=-\frac{1}{2},\quad{\rm Im}\,\lambda_{\pm}=\pm|\xi|\sqrt{1-\frac{1}{4|\xi|^{2}}}.

(ii)
For
|\xi|<\frac{1}{2}
,

e^{-t\hat{A}_{\xi}}
has the spectral resolution

\displaystyle e^{-t\hat{A}_{\xi}}=\sum_{j=\pm}e^{t\lambda_{j}(\xi)}{\Pi}_{j}(\xi),

where
{\Pi}_{j}(\xi)
is eigenprojections for
\lambda_{j}(\xi)\,(\pm)
, and
\Pi_{j}(\xi)\,(\pm)
satisfy

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&I_{d}-\frac{\xi{}^{\top}{\xi}}{|\xi|^{2}}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\Pi_{\pm}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\pm\frac{1}{\lambda_{+}-\lambda_{-}}\begin{pmatrix}-\lambda_{\mp}&-i{}^{\top}{\xi}\\
-i\xi&\lambda_{\pm}\frac{\xi{}^{\top}{\xi}}{|\xi|^{2}}\end{pmatrix}.

Furthermore, if
0<r_{\infty}<\frac{1}{2}
,
then there exist a constant
C>0
such that the estimates

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{j}(\xi)\|\leq C\,(j=0,\pm)

(5.6)

hold for
|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}
.

Hereafter we fix
0<r_{1}<r_{\infty}<\frac{1}{2}

so that
(\ref{Pi})
in Lemma
5.3
holds for
|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\alpha
be a multi-index.
Then the following estimates hold true uniformly for
\xi
with
|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}
and
t\in[0,T]
.

(i)

|\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}\lambda_{0}|\leq C|\xi|^{-|\alpha|}
,
|\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}\lambda_{+}|\leq C|\xi|^{2-|\alpha|}
,

|\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}\lambda_{-}|\leq C|\xi|^{-|\alpha|}

(|\alpha|\geq 0)
.

(ii)

|(\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}\Pi_{0})\hat{F}_{1}|\leq C|\xi|^{-|\alpha|}|\hat{F}^{2}_{1}|
,

|(\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}\Pi_{\pm})\hat{F}_{1}|\leq C|\xi|^{-|\alpha|}|\hat{F}_{1}|

(|\alpha|\geq 0)
,
where
{F}_{1}={}^{\top}(F^{1}_{1},{F}^{2}_{1})
.

(iii)

|\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}(e^{\lambda_{\pm}t})|\leq C|\xi|^{-|\alpha|}

(|\alpha|\geq 0)
.

(iv)

|(\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}e^{-t\hat{A}_{\xi}})\hat{F}_{1}|\leq C(|\xi|^{-|\alpha|}|\hat{F}_{1}^{1}|+|\xi|^{-|\alpha|}|\hat{F}^{2}_{1}|)

(|\alpha|\geq 1)
,
where
{F}_{1}={}^{\top}(F^{1}_{1},{F}^{2}_{1})
.

Lemma
5.4
can be verified by direct computations based on Lemma
\ref{eigenvalue}.

We set

(I-e^{\lambda_{k}T})^{-1}\Pi_{k}(\xi):=\begin{pmatrix}E_{k,11}(\xi)&E_{k,12}(\xi)\\
E_{k,21}(\xi)&E_{k,22}(\xi)\end{pmatrix}.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\alpha
be a multi-index.
Then the following estimates hold true uniformly for
\xi
with
|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}
and
t\in[0,T]
.

(i)

|\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}E_{+,11}\hat{F}_{1}|\leq C|\xi|^{-2-|\alpha|}|\hat{F}_{1}|

(|\alpha|\geq 0)

(ii)

|\partial_{\xi}^{\alpha}E_{k,ij}\hat{F}_{1}|\leq C|\xi|^{-1-|\alpha|}|\hat{F}_{1}|

(|\alpha|\geq 0)
, where
k=0,\pm
and
ij\neq 11
for
k=+
.

Lemma
5.5
can also be verified by direct computations based on Lemma
\ref{eigenvalue}.
We note that

(I-e^{\lambda_{k}T})^{-1}\Pi_{k}\sim\dfrac{1}{\lambda_{k}T}\Pi_{k}

as
|\xi|\rightarrow 0
by the Taylor expansion of
e^{\lambda_{k}T}
.

Set

\displaystyle E_{1,k}(x):={\cal F}^{-1}(\hat{\chi}_{0}(I-e^{\lambda_{k}T})^{-1}\Pi_{j})\ \ (k=0,\pm),\ \ \mbox{$(x\in\mathbb{R}^{d})$}

where
\chi_{0}
is the cut-off function defined by
(\ref{subcutofflowpart})
. In addition, we write as

\displaystyle E_{1,k}(x):=\begin{pmatrix}E_{1,k,11}&E_{1,k,12}\\
E_{1,k,21}&E_{1,k,22}\end{pmatrix},

where
E_{1,k,ij}={\cal F}^{-1}(\hat{\chi}_{0}E_{k,ij})
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\alpha
be a multi-index satisfying
|\alpha|\geq 0
.
Then the following estimates hold true uniformly for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

(i)

|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{1,+,11}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)^{-(d-2+|\alpha|)}.

(ii)

|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{1,k,ij}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)^{-(d-1+|\alpha|)}
, where
k=0,\pm
and
ij\neq 11
for
k=+
.

Proof.
It follows from Lemma
5.4
that

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{1,k}(x)|\leq C\int_{|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}}|\xi|^{-2}d\xi\ \ \mbox{$(x\in\mathbb{R}^{d})$}.

Since
\int_{|\xi|\leq r_{\infty}}|\xi|^{-2}d\xi<\infty
for
d\geq 3
,
we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{k}|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{1,k}(x)|\leq C\ \ \mbox{$(x\in\mathbb{R}^{d})$},

(5.7)

where
C>0
is a constant depending on
\alpha
,
T
and
d
.
By Lemma
5.4
, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}((i\xi)^{\alpha}\hat{\chi}_{0}E_{+,11}(\xi)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\xi|^{-2+|\alpha|-|\beta|}\ \ \mbox{for}\ |\beta|\geq 0,

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}((i\xi)^{\alpha}\hat{\chi}_{0}E_{k,ij}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\xi|^{-1+|\alpha|-|\beta|}\ \ \mbox{for}\ |\beta|\geq 0,

where
k=0,\pm
and
ij\neq 11
for
k=+
.
It then follows from Lemma
4.5
that

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{1,+,11}(x)|\leq C|x|^{-(d-2+|\alpha|)}\ \ \mbox{and}\ \ |\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{1,k,ij}(x)|\leq C|x|^{-(d-1+|\alpha|)}.

(5.8)

From
(\ref{inverseconstant})
and
(\ref{inversemugenennpou})
, we obtain the desired estimates.
This completes the proof.
\hfill\square

Now we are in a position to prove Proposition
5.2
.

Proof of Proposition

5.2
.

We define a function
u
by

\displaystyle u=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(I-e^{-T\hat{A}_{\xi}})^{-1}\hat{F}_{1}.

(i)
By using Lemma
5.4
, one can easily obtain
(\ref{inverseL2estimate1})
.
As for
(\ref{inveeseLinftyestimate1})
, note that

\displaystyle u=\mathcal{F}^{-1}((I-e^{-T\hat{A}_{\xi}})^{-1}\hat{F}_{1})=\sum_{k}E_{1,k}\ast F_{1},

where
E_{1,k}
is the ones defined in Lemma
5.6
. We can rewrite by

\displaystyle\sum_{k}E_{1,k}\ast F_{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}E_{k,11}\ast{F}^{1}_{1}+\sum_{k}E_{k,12}\ast{F}^{2}_{1}\\
\sum_{k}E_{k,21}\ast{F}^{1}_{1}+\sum_{k}E_{k,22}\ast{F}^{2}_{1}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\Big[\hat{\chi}_{0}\begin{pmatrix}\sum_{k}E_{k,11}\hat{F}^{1}_{1}+\sum_{k}E_{k,12}\hat{F}^{2}_{1}\\
\sum_{k}E_{k,21}\hat{F}^{1}_{1}+\sum_{k}E_{k,22}\hat{F}^{2}_{1}\end{pmatrix}\Big]

for
F_{1}={}^{\top}(F_{1}^{1},F_{1}^{2})
.
Then by Lemma
5.6
, we see that
\sum_{k}E_{1,k,ij}
satisfies

|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{1,+,11}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)^{-(d-2+|\alpha|)}\ \ (|\alpha|\geq 0)

and

|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\sum_{k}E_{1,k,ij}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)^{-(d-1+|\alpha|)}\ \ (|\alpha|\geq 0),

where
k=0,\pm
and
ij\neq 11
for
k=+
.
Therefore, applying Lemma
4.6
(i), we obtain
(\ref{inveeseLinftyestimate1})
.

The assertion
{\rm(ii)}
similarly follows from Lemma
4.6
(ii), Lemma
5.4
and Lemma
5.6
.

{\rm(iii)}
By using Lemma
5.4
, one can easily obtain
(\ref{inverseL2estimate3})
.
As for
(\ref{inveeseLinftyestimate3})
,
if there exists a function
F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1)}\cap L^{\infty}_{d}
satisfying
F_{1}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F^{(1)}_{1}

for some
\alpha
satisfying
|\alpha|\geq 1
, then

\displaystyle u=\left(\sum_{k}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{1,k}\right)\ast F^{(1)}_{1}.

Lemma
5.6
yields

\displaystyle|\sum_{k}\partial_{x}^{\alpha+\beta}E_{1,k}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)^{-(d-1+|\beta|)}

for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
|\alpha|\geq 1
and
|\beta|\geq 0
.
It then follows from Lemma
4.6
(iii) that

\|u\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)},L^{\infty}}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}.

This completes the proof.
\hfill\square

In view of Proposition
5.2
(i),
I-S_{1}(T)
has a bounded inverse

:

and
it holds that

\displaystyle\|(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}\leq C\{\|F_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}+\|F_{1}\|_{L^{1}}\},

\displaystyle\|(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}\leq C(\|F_{1}\|_{L^{1}}+\|F_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{1}}).

If
{F}_{1}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{(1),1}

for some
\alpha
satisfying

, then

\displaystyle\|(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}\leq C\{\|F_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}+\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d-1}}\},

\displaystyle\|(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}\leq C(\|F_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{1}}+\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{2}}).

Furthermore, if
{F}_{1}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1)}
with

for some
\alpha
satisfying
|\alpha|\geq 1
, then
(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}F_{1}\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}
and

\displaystyle\|(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}},

\displaystyle\|(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{1}}.

We next estimate
S_{1}(t){\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(T)(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}F_{1}
and
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)F_{1}
.
Let
E_{1}(t,\sigma)
and

be defined by

\displaystyle E_{1}(t,\sigma)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\{\hat{\chi}_{0}e^{-t\hat{A}_{\xi}}(I-e^{-T\hat{A}_{\xi}})^{-1}e^{-(T-\sigma)\hat{A}_{\xi}}\},

\displaystyle E_{2}(t,\tau)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\{\hat{\chi}_{0}e^{-(t-\tau)\hat{A}_{\xi}}\}

for

, where

Then
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)F_{1}
and

are given by

\displaystyle S_{1}(t){\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(T)(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}F_{1}=\int_{0}^{T}E_{1}(t,\sigma)\ast F_{1}(\sigma)d\sigma

(5.9)

\displaystyle{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)F_{1}=\int_{0}^{t}S_{1}(t-\tau)F_{1}(\tau)d\tau=\int_{0}^{t}E_{2}(t,\tau)\ast F_{1}(\tau)d\tau.

(5.10)

We have the following estimates for
E_{1}(t,\sigma)\ast F_{1}
and
E_{2}(t,\tau)\ast F_{1}
.

Lemma 5.7
.

If
{F}_{1}
satisfies the conditions given in
{\rm(i)}
-
{\rm(iii)}
, then,
E_{1}(t,\sigma)\ast F_{1}\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}
,
E_{2}(t,\tau)\ast F_{1}\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}

(t,\sigma,\tau\in[0,T],j=1,2)

and
E_{1}(t,\sigma)\ast F_{1},E_{2}(t,\tau)\ast F_{1}
satisfy the estimates in each case of
{\rm(i)}
-
{\rm(iii)}
.

{\rm(i)}

{F}_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1),1}\cap L^{\infty}\cap L^{1}
;

\|E_{1}(t,\sigma)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}+\|E_{2}(t,\tau)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}\leq C\{\|F_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}+\|F_{1}\|_{L^{1}}\}

and

\|E_{1}(t,\sigma)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}+\|E_{2}(t,\tau)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}\leq C(\|F_{1}\|_{L^{1}}+\|F_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{1}})

uniformly for
\sigma\in[0,T]
and
0\leq\tau\leq t\leq T
.

{\rm(ii)}

{F}_{1}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{(1),1}
with
F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1)}\cap L^{\infty}_{d-1}

for some
\alpha
satisfying
|\alpha|=1
;

\|E_{1}(t,\sigma)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}+\|E_{2}(t,\tau)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}\leq C\{\|F_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}+\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d-1}}\}

and

\|E_{1}(t,\sigma)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}+\|E_{2}(t,\tau)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}\leq C(\|F_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{1}}+\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{2}})

uniformly for
\sigma\in[0,T]
and
0\leq\tau\leq t\leq T
.

{\rm(iii)}

{F}_{1}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1)}
with
F^{(1)}_{1}\in L^{2}_{(1),1}\cap L^{\infty}_{d}

for some
\alpha
satisfying
|\alpha|\geq 1
;

\|E_{1}(t,\sigma)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}+\|E_{2}(t,\tau)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}}}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d}}

and

\|E_{1}(t,\sigma)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}+\|E_{2}(t,\tau)\ast F_{1}\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{2}_{1}}

uniformly for
\sigma\in[0,T]
and
0\leq\tau\leq t\leq T
.

Proof of Lemma

5.7
.

By Lemmas
5.3
and
5.4
, we see that

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}(\hat{\chi}_{0}(i\xi)^{\alpha}e^{-t\hat{A}_{\xi}}(I-e^{-T\hat{A}_{\xi}})^{-1}e^{-(T-\sigma)\hat{A}_{\xi}})|\leq C|\xi|^{-2+|\alpha|-|\beta|},

\displaystyle|\partial_{\xi}^{\beta}(\hat{\chi}_{0}(i\xi)^{\alpha}e^{-(t-\tau)\hat{A}_{\xi}})|\leq C|\xi|^{|\alpha|-|\beta|}

for
\sigma\in[0,T]
,
0\leq\tau\leq t\leq T
and
|\beta|\geq 0
. It then follows from Lemma
4.5
that

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{1}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)^{-(d-2+|\alpha|)},\ \ |\partial_{x}^{\alpha}E_{2}(x)|\leq C(1+|x|)^{-(d+|\alpha|)}

(5.11)

for
|\alpha|\geq 0
.
Therefore, in a similarly manner to the proof of Proposition
5.2
, we obtain the desired estimate by using Lemma
4.6
and Lemma
5.6
. This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

We see from Proposition
5.1
(i), (ii) and Lemma
5.7
that
the following estimates hold for
S_{1}(t){\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(T)(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}
and
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)
.

.

(5.12)

where

\tilde{F_{1}}\end{pmatrix}
or

If

satisfies the conditions given in

-
{\rm(iii)}

-

for

.

{\rm(i)}

;

\|\Gamma_{1}[\tilde{F_{1}}]\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}\leq C\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{1}\cap L^{2}_{1})},

\|\partial_{t}\Gamma_{1}[\tilde{F_{1}}]\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)})\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}\leq C\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{1}\cap L^{2}_{1})}

and

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{2}[\tilde{F_{1}}]\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}\leq C\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{1}\cap L^{2}_{1})},

{\rm(ii)}

\tilde{F}_{1}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F^{(1)}_{1}\in C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{(1),1}\cap{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})

for some

satisfying

;

\|\partial_{t}\Gamma_{1}[\tilde{F_{1}}]\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)})\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}\leq C(\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{1})}+\|{F}^{(1)}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d-1}\cap L^{2})})

and

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{2}[\tilde{F_{1}}]\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{1})}+\|{F}^{(1)}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d-1}\cap L^{2})}),

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\Gamma_{2}[\tilde{F_{1}}]\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)})\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{1})}+\|{F}^{(1)}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d-1}\cap L^{2})}

\displaystyle+\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}).

{\rm(iii)}

with

for some

satisfying

;

\|\Gamma_{1}[\tilde{F_{1}}]\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{1})},

\|\partial_{t}\Gamma_{1}[\tilde{F_{1}}]\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)})\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{1})}

and

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{2}[\tilde{F_{1}}]\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{1})},

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\Gamma_{2}[\tilde{F_{1}}]\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)})\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}\leq C(\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{1})}+\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})}).

As for
\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{p}})}

(p=2,\infty)
, we have the following proposition.

Proposition 5.9
.

If
\tilde{F}_{1}
satisfies the conditions given in
{\rm(i)}
-
{\rm(iii)}
, then,

\tilde{F}_{1}\in C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)})
and

-

.

{\rm(i)}

;

\displaystyle\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),\infty}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}})}\leq C\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{1})},

\displaystyle\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}})}\leq C\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}\cap L^{2}_{1})}.

{\rm(ii)}

\tilde{F}_{1}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F^{(1)}_{1}\in C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{d}\cap L^{2}_{(1),1})
with

for some
\alpha
satisfying

;

\displaystyle\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),\infty}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}})}\leq C(\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}_{d})}+\|{F}^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}_{d-1})}),

\displaystyle\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}})}\leq C(\|{F}^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2})}+\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}_{1})}).

{\rm(iii)}

\tilde{F}_{1}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F^{(1)}_{1}\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{(1)})
with

for some

satisfying

;

\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),\infty}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{\infty}})}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{\infty}_{d})},

\|\tilde{F}_{1}\|_{C([0,T];{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1),L^{2}})}\leq C\|F^{(1)}_{1}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}_{1})}.

Proof of Proposition

5.9
.

We see that
\tilde{F}_{1}=\chi_{0}\ast F_{1}
, where
\chi_{0}=\mathcal{F}^{-1}\hat{\chi}_{0}
,
\hat{\chi}_{0}
is the cut-off function defined by
(\ref{subcutofflowpart})
satisfying
(\ref{E_0estimate})
.
Therefore, in a similar manner to the proof of Proposition
5.2
, we obtain the desired estimates.
This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

We are now in a position to give estimates for a solution of (

) satisfying
u_{1}(0)=u_{1}(T)
.

For
\mathbb{F}^{1}=\begin{pmatrix}0\\

\Gamma[\tilde{F}_{1}]=S_{1}(t){\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(T)(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}\mathbb{F}^{1}+{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)\mathbb{F}^{1}.

Then
\Gamma[\tilde{F}_{1}]

(5.13)

where

and

are the ones defined by
(\ref{kaisayousononlinearhyougenn})
.

Proposition 5.10
.

If
\tilde{F}_{1}

-

, then,

) with

.

;

(5.14)

{\rm(ii)}

(5.16)

Proof.

) with

.

6
Properties of
S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(t)
and
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(t)

In this section we mainly investigate some properties of
S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(t)
and
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(t)
in weighted Sobolev spaces.

First of all, we consider local existence of the following linear system.

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}a_{\infty}+\text{div}v_{\infty}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla a_{\infty}=F_{\infty}^{0},\\
&\partial_{t}v_{\infty}+\nabla a_{\infty}+v_{\infty}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\infty}+g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\cdot\nabla a_{\infty}=F_{\infty}^{1},\\
&(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})|_{t=0}=(a_{0\infty},v_{0\infty}).\end{aligned}\right.

(6.1)

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
d\geq 3
and
s
be an integer satisfying
s\geq[\frac{d}{2}]+2
. Assume that
\nabla\tilde{\phi}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1}),~\tilde{v}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})
with

\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}\leq 1,

F_{\infty}={}^{\top}{(F_{\infty}^{0},F_{\infty}^{1})}\in L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H_{(\infty)}^{s})\cap C(0,T^{\prime};H_{(\infty)}^{s-1})
and
{}^{\top}(a_{0\infty},v_{0\infty})\in H_{(\infty)}^{s}
. Here
T^{\prime}
is a given positive number. Then (
6.1
) has a unique solution
{}^{\top}(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H_{(\infty)}^{s})
satisfying that

\|{}^{\top}(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})\|_{C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})}\leq C\|{}^{\top}(a_{0\infty},v_{0\infty})\|_{H^{s}}+\|F_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H^{s})\cap C(0,T^{\prime};H^{s-1})},

where
C>0
depends on
T^{\prime}
.

Since

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}(\|g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})(t)\|_{H^{s-1}})\leq C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T^{\prime}}\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}(t)\|_{H^{s-1}}\leq C,

and
F_{\infty}\in L^{1}([0,T^{\prime}];H_{(\infty)}^{s})\cap C([0,T^{\prime}];H_{(\infty)}^{s-1})
for a given
T^{\prime}
,
Lemma
6.1
follows directly by
[
13
, Theorem I]
.

At the beginning, we introduce the solvability of (
4
).

6.1
Solvability of the system (
4
)

We begin with the solvability of the following system for the density,

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}a_{\infty}+P_{\infty}(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla a_{\infty})=F_{\infty}^{0},\\
&a_{\infty}|_{t=0}=a_{0\infty}.\end{aligned}\right.

(6.2)

After taking a same method developed in Lemma 6.2 in
[
10
]
, we obtain the following solvability of (
6.2
)

Lemma 6.2
.

Let
d\geq 3
and
s
be an integer satisfying
s\geq[\frac{d}{2}]+2
. Set
k=s-1
or
s
. Assume that
\tilde{v}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})\cap L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H^{s})
,

F_{\infty}^{0}\in L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H_{(\infty)}^{k})\cap C([0,T^{\prime}];H_{(\infty)}^{k-1})
and
a_{0\infty}\in H^{k}_{(\infty)}
. Here
T^{\prime}
is a given positive number. Then (
6.2
) has a unique solution
a_{\infty}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H_{(\infty)}^{k})
and
a_{\infty}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|a_{\infty}(t)\|_{H^{k}}^{2}\leq C\Big\{\|a_{0\infty}\|_{H^{k}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}(\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}+\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}^{2})\|a_{\infty}(\tau)\|_{H^{k}}^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{\infty}^{0}(\tau)\|_{H^{k}}\|a(\tau)\|_{H^{k}}d\tau\Big\},

(6.3)

and

\displaystyle\|a_{\infty}(t)\|_{H^{k}}^{2}\leq Ce^{C\int_{0}^{t}(1+\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}+\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}^{2})d\tau}\Big\{\|a_{0\infty}\|_{H^{k}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{\infty}^{0}(\tau)\|_{H^{k}}^{2}d\tau\Big\},

for
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
.

Next, we prove the solvability of the following system of the velocity,

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}v_{\infty}+v_{\infty}+P_{\infty}(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\infty})=F_{\infty}^{1},\\
&v_{\infty}|_{t=0}=v_{0\infty}.\end{aligned}\right.

(6.4)

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
d\geq 3
and
s
be an integer satisfying
s\geq[\frac{d}{2}]+2
. Assume that
\tilde{v}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})\cap L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H^{s})
,
F_{\infty}^{1}\in L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H_{(\infty)}^{s})\cap C([0,T^{\prime}];H_{(\infty)}^{s-1})
and
v_{0\infty}\in H_{(\infty)}^{s}
. Here
T^{\prime}
is a given positive number. Then (
6.4
) admits a unique solution
v_{\infty}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H_{(\infty)}^{s})\cap L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H_{(\infty)}^{s})\cap H^{1}(0,T^{\prime};H_{(\infty)}^{s-1})
satisfying

\displaystyle\|v_{\infty}(t)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\leq C\Big\{\|v_{0\infty}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\|v_{\infty}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}d\tau

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{\infty}^{1}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}d\tau\Big\},

(6.5)

and

\displaystyle\|v_{\infty}(t)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\leq Ce^{C\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}d\tau}\Big\{\|v_{0\infty}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{\infty}^{1}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}d\tau\Big\}

(6.6)

for
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
.

Proof.

Define
v_{\infty}^{(0)}=0
. Assume that
v_{\infty}^{(n)}(n\geq 1)
satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}v_{\infty}^{(n)}+v_{\infty}^{(n)}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\infty}^{(n)}=F_{\infty}^{1}+P_{1}(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\infty}^{(n-1)}),\\
&v_{\infty}^{(n)}|_{t=0}=v_{0\infty}.\end{aligned}\right.

Then we could derive the equation of
(v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)})
as follows,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)})+v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla(v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)})

\displaystyle=P_{1}(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla(v_{\infty}^{(n)}-v_{\infty}^{(n-1)})),

and the initial data satisfy

\displaystyle(v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)})|_{t=0}=0.

Let
s\geq[\frac{d}{2}]+2
. Taking
L^{2}
energy estimates on the above equations and using Hölder inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}\|v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}\|v_{\infty}^{(n)}-v_{\infty}^{(n-1)}\|_{H^{s}}\|v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}\|_{H^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}\|v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\|v_{\infty}^{(n)}-v_{\infty}^{(n-1)}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\varepsilon\|v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

Due to the smallness of
\varepsilon
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}\|v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\|v_{\infty}^{(n)}-v_{\infty}^{(n-1)}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

In terms of Grönwall’s inequality, one has

\displaystyle\|v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}(t)-v_{\infty}^{(n)}(t)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\leq M_{0}\frac{(M_{1}t)^{n+1}}{(n+1)!}\quad(n\geq 0),

where

\displaystyle M_{0}=e^{C\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}\|\tilde{v}(t)\|_{H^{s}}dt}\Big\{\|v_{0\infty}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}\|F_{\infty}^{1}(t)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}dt\Big\},

(6.7)

and

\displaystyle M_{1}=C\|\tilde{v}\|_{C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})}^{2}e^{C\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}\|\tilde{v}(t)\|_{H^{s}}dt}.

(6.8)

Therefore, one can see that
v_{\infty}^{(n)}
converges in
C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})
to a function
v_{\infty}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})
that satisfies

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}v_{\infty}+v_{\infty}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\infty}=F_{\infty}^{1}+P_{1}(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\infty}),\\
&v_{\infty}|_{t=0}=v_{0\infty}.\end{aligned}\right.

Hence
v_{\infty}
is a solution of (
6.4
). Meanwhile, it holds that

\displaystyle\|v_{\infty}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\leq Ce^{C\int_{0}^{t}\|\tilde{v}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}d\tau}(\|v_{0\infty}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\int_{0}^{t}\|F_{\infty}(\tau)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}d\tau).

(6.9)

Since the initial data
\mbox{\rm supp}\,\hat{v}_{0\infty}(t)\subset\{|\xi|\geq r_{1}\}
, according to the above estimates, we can prove
\mbox{\rm supp}\,\hat{v}_{\infty}(t)\subset\{|\xi|\geq r_{1}\}
for
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
. Therefore we complete the proof.

\hfill\square

With the Lemmas
6.3
and
6.2
in hands, we are able to prove the solvability of (
6.17
).

Proposition 6.4
.

Let
d\geq 3
and
s
be an integer satisfying
s\geq[\frac{d}{2}]+2.

Assume that

\displaystyle\nabla\tilde{\phi}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1})\cap L^{2}([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1}),~\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1})

\displaystyle\tilde{v}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})\cap L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H^{s}),

\displaystyle u_{0\infty}\in H^{s}_{(\infty)},\quad F_{\infty}={}^{\top}{(F_{\infty}^{0},F_{\infty}^{1})}\in L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H^{s}_{(\infty)})\cap C([0,T^{\prime}];H_{(\infty)}^{s-1}).

(6.10)

Here
T^{\prime}
is a given positive number.
Then there exists a unique solution

u_{\infty}={}^{\top}{(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})}
of (
6.17
) satisfying

\displaystyle a_{\infty}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s}_{(\infty)}),v_{\infty}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s}_{(\infty)})\cap L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H^{s}_{(\infty)})\cap H^{1}(0,T^{\prime};H^{s-1}_{(\infty)}).

(6.11)

Proof.

We define
u_{\infty}^{(n)}={}^{\top}{(a_{\infty}^{(n)},v_{\infty}^{(n)})},n=0,1,2,...
For
n=0
,
v_{\infty}^{(0)}=0
and
a_{\infty}^{(0)}
is the solution of

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}a_{\infty}^{(0)}+P_{\infty}(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla a_{\infty}^{(0)})=F_{\infty}^{0},\\
&a_{\infty}^{(0)}|_{t=0}=a_{0\infty}.\end{aligned}\right.

(6.12)

For
n\geq 1
,
{}^{\top}{(a_{\infty}^{(n)},v_{\infty}^{(n)})}
is the solution of

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}a_{\infty}^{(n)}+\text{div}v_{\infty}^{(n)}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla a_{\infty}^{(n)}=F_{\infty}^{0}+P_{1}(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla a_{\infty}^{(n-1)}),\\
&a_{\infty}^{(n)}|_{t=0}=a_{0\infty},\end{aligned}\right.

(6.13)

and

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}v_{\infty}^{(n)}+\nabla a_{\infty}^{(n)}+v_{\infty}^{(n)}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\infty}^{(n)}+g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\nabla a_{\infty}^{(n)}\\
&\quad=F_{\infty}^{1}+P_{1}(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\infty}^{(n-1)})+P_{1}(g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\nabla v_{\infty}^{(n-1)}),\\
&v_{\infty}^{(0)}|_{t=0}=v_{0\infty}.\end{aligned}\right.

(6.14)

Then we can get the equation of
{}^{\top}{(a_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-a_{\infty}^{(n)},v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)})}
as follows,

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}(a_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-a_{\infty}^{(n)})+\text{div}(v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)})+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla(a_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-a_{\infty}^{(n)})=P_{1}(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla(a_{\infty}^{(n)}-a_{\infty}^{(n-1)})),\\
&\partial_{t}(v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)})+\nabla(a_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-a_{\infty}^{(n)})+v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla(v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)})\\
&+g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\nabla(a_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-a_{\infty}^{(n)})=P_{1}(\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla(v_{\infty}^{(n)}-v_{\infty}^{(n-1)}))+P_{1}(g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\nabla(v_{\infty}^{(n)}-v_{\infty}^{(n-1)})),\end{aligned}\right.

with the initial data

\displaystyle(a_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-a_{\infty}^{(n)},v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)})|_{t=0}=(0,0).

Then by
L^{2}
energy method, one has

\displaystyle\|(a_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-a_{\infty}^{(n)},v_{\infty}^{(n+1)}-v_{\infty}^{(n)})\|_{H^{s}}^{2}\leq M_{3}\frac{(M_{2}t)^{n+1}}{(n+1)!},

where

\displaystyle M_{2}=e^{C\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}(\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}(t)\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\tilde{v}(t)\|_{H^{s}})dt}

\displaystyle\Big\{\|a_{0\infty}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|v_{0\infty}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}(\|F_{\infty}^{0}(t)\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\|F_{\infty}^{1}(t)\|_{H^{s}}^{2})dt\Big\},

(6.15)

and

\displaystyle M_{3}=C(\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1})}^{2}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})}^{2})e^{C\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}(\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}(t)\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\tilde{v}(t)\|_{H^{s}})dt}.

(6.16)

To this end, one can show that
u_{\infty}^{(n)}={}^{\top}{(a_{\infty}^{(n)},v_{\infty}^{(n)})}
converges to a pair of function
u_{\infty}={}^{\top}{(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})}
in
C([0,T^{\prime}];H_{(\infty)}^{s})
. It is not difficult to see that

u_{\infty}={}^{\top}{(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})}
is a unique solution of (
6.17
). This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

6.2
Estimates of operators
S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(t)
and
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(t)

We first consider the following equation

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}a_{\infty}+\text{div}v_{\infty}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla a_{\infty}=F_{\infty}^{0},\\
&\partial_{t}v_{\infty}+\nabla a_{\infty}+v_{\infty}+\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\infty}+g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\cdot\nabla a_{\infty}=F_{\infty}^{1},\\
&(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})|_{t=0}=(a_{0\infty},v_{0\infty}).\end{aligned}\right.

(6.17)

For nonnegative integers
k
and
\ell
, we define
|f|_{H_{j}^{k}}
by

\displaystyle|f|_{H_{j}^{k}}=\sum_{|\alpha|=0}^{k}\Big\||x|^{j}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f\Big\|_{L^{2}}.

For simplicity, we introduce the energy
E_{j}^{k}[u_{\infty}](t)
and dissipation
D_{j}^{k}[u_{\infty}](t)
as follows

\displaystyle E_{j}^{k}[u_{\infty}](t)

\displaystyle=|a_{\infty}|_{H_{j}^{k}}^{2}+|v_{\infty}|_{H_{j}^{k}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{|\alpha|=0}^{k}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\big||x|^{j}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\eta_{R}a_{\infty})\big|^{2}\mathrm{d}x,

(6.18)

\displaystyle D_{j}^{k}[u_{\infty}](t)

\displaystyle=|\nabla a_{\infty}|_{H_{j}^{k-1}}^{2}+|v_{\infty}|_{H_{j}^{k}}^{2}.

(6.19)

In terms of the Poincaré type inequality for the high-frequency part of the solution, i.e., Lemma
4.9
with that
\|g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C
, we have

E_{j}^{k}[u_{\infty}](t)\leq CD_{j}^{k}[u_{\infty}](t),

(6.20)

for some constant
C>0
.

First, we establish the energy estimates of
E_{0}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)
and
D_{0}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)
.

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
d\geq 3
and
s
be a nonnegative integer satisfying
s\geq[\frac{d}{2}]+2
. Assume that

\displaystyle\nabla\tilde{\phi}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1})\cap L^{2}([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1}),~\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}\in C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1})

\displaystyle\tilde{v}\in C([0,T];H^{s})\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{s}),

\displaystyle u_{0\infty}={}^{\top}(a_{0\infty},v_{0\infty})\in H_{(\infty)}^{s},

\displaystyle F_{\infty}={}^{\top}(F_{\infty}^{0},F_{\infty}^{1})\in L^{2}(0,T^{\prime};H^{s})\cap C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1}).

Then the initial value problem (
6.17
) exists a unique solution
u_{\infty}={}^{\top}(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})
in
C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})\cap L^{2}(0,T;H^{s})
.

Furthermore, there exist a positive constant
\kappa_{0}
such that the estimates

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}E_{0}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)+\kappa_{0}D_{0}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon\|a_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}^{2})\|u_{\infty}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\|F_{\infty}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}

(6.21)

holds on
t\in(0,T^{\prime})
with
T^{\prime}
a given positive number, where
C>0
is a constant independent of time.

Proof.

The existence is due to
[
13
, Theorem I]
as in the Proposition
6.4
.
We mainly focus the proof of (
6.21
).

Multiplying (
6.17
) by
u_{\infty}
, and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by parts then adding them together, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\|a_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|v_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})+\|v_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(-\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla a_{\infty}a_{\infty}-\tilde{v}\cdot\nabla v_{\infty}\cdot v_{\infty}-g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\nabla a_{\infty}\cdot v_{\infty}+F_{\infty}^{0}a_{\infty}+F_{\infty}^{1}\cdot v_{\infty})\mathrm{d}x.

(6.22)

By Hölder inequality, integration by parts and Sobolev inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\|a_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|v_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2})+\|v_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big\{\|\text{div}\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|a_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\text{div}\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|v_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|g_{3}(\tilde{\phi})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla a_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}\|v_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|F_{\infty}^{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|a_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}+\|F_{\infty}^{1}\|_{L^{2}}\|v_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}}\Big\}
