\displaystyle M_{4}=C\Big\{\|u_{0\infty}\|_{H_{1}^{s}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T^{\prime}}\|F_{\infty}\|_{H_{\ell}^{s}}^{2}d\tau\Big\}e^{C\|b\|_{L^{1}(0,T^{\prime})}}.

Similarly, using (
6.60
) with
U_{\infty}=U_{\infty}^{(n)}-U_{\infty}^{(n-1)}
and
U=U_{\infty}^{(n-1)}-u_{\infty}^{(n-2)}
for
n=2,3,...
, one can inductively show that

\displaystyle\|U_{\infty}^{(n)}(t)-U_{\infty}^{(n-1)}\|_{H_{\ell}^{s}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{4}(CK_{0}\delta^{2}t)^{n-1}}{(n-1)!},

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|U_{\infty}^{(n)}(t)-U_{\infty}^{(n-1)}\|_{H_{\ell}^{s}}d\tau

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{4}}{K_{0}}\Big\{\frac{(CK_{0}\delta^{2}t)^{n-1}}{(n-1)!}+\frac{\|b\|_{L^{1}(0,T^{\prime})}}{\delta^{2}}\frac{(CK_{0}\delta^{2}t)^{n}}{n!}.\Big\}

Here
K_{0}=1+\|b\|_{L^{1}(0,T^{\prime})}Ce^{C\|b\|_{L^{1}(0,T^{\prime})}}
. It then follows that
U_{\infty}^{(n)}
converges to a function
U_{\infty}
in
C([0,T^{\prime}];H_{\ell}^{s})\cap L^{2}([0,T^{\prime}];H_{\ell}^{s})
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. One can easily see that
U_{\infty}
satisfies (
6.59
) with
U=U_{\infty}
, i.e.,
U_{\infty}
is a solution of (
4
) and
U_{\infty}\in H_{(\infty)}^{s}
for all
t\in[0,T^{\prime}]
. By the uniqueness of solutions of (
4
), we see that
U_{\infty}=u_{\infty}
.

It holds

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}E_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)+\kappa_{2}D_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}^{2})|u_{\infty}|_{H_{\ell}^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle+C|F_{\infty}|_{H_{\ell}^{s}}^{2}+C|u_{\infty}|_{H_{\ell-1}^{s}}^{2},

(6.61)

and

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}E_{0}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)+\kappa_{0}D_{0}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}^{2})\|u_{\infty}\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\|F_{\infty}\|^{2}_{H^{s}}.

(6.62)

We now prove (
6.58
) by induction on
\ell
. When
\ell=0
, it is true by (
6.62
). Assume that (
6.58
) holds for
\ell=j-1
. Then by adding

\frac{\kappa}{2C}\times\eqref{112703}
to (
6.58
) with
\ell=j-1
, we obtain that there exists a positive constant
\kappa
such that the desired inequality (
6.58
) for
\ell=j
holds, where
\mathcal{E}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)=E_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)+\frac{\kappa}{2C}E_{0}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)
.

It is directly shown that
\mathcal{E}^{s}[u_{\infty}]
is equivalent to
\|u_{\infty}\|_{H_{\ell}^{s}}^{2}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle C^{-1}\|u_{\infty}\|_{H_{\ell}^{s}}\leq\mathcal{E}^{s}[u_{\infty}]\leq C\|u_{\infty}\|_{H_{\ell}^{s}}^{2}.

This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

The operators
S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(t)
and
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(t)
have the following properties.

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
d\geq 3
and
s
be a nonnegative integer satisfying
s\geq[\frac{d}{2}]+2
.
Let
\ell
be a nonnegative integer.
Assume that
\tilde{u}={}^{\top}(\tilde{a},\tilde{v})
satisfies (
6.55
).
Then there exists a constant
\delta>0
such that the following assertions hold true
if

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}\|_{C([0,T];H^{s-1})}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{C([0,T];H^{s-1})}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{C([0,T];H^{s})}\leq\delta,

(6.63)

(i)

It holds that
S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(\cdot)u_{0\infty}\in C([0,\infty);H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s})

for each
u_{0\infty}={}^{\top}(a_{0\infty},v_{0\infty})\in H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}

and there exist constants
c_{1}>0
and
C>0
such that

S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(t)
satisfies the estimate

\displaystyle\|S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(t)u_{0\infty}\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}}\leq Ce^{-c_{1}t}\|u_{0\infty}\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}}

for all
t\geq 0
and
u_{0\infty}\in H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}
.

(ii)

It holds that
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(\cdot)F_{\infty}\in C([0,T];H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s})

for each

F_{\infty}={}^{\top}(F_{\infty}^{0},F_{\infty}^{1})\in L^{2}(0,T;H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s})\cap C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1}_{(\infty),\ell})

and
{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(t)
satisfies the estimate

\displaystyle\|{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(t)[F_{\infty}]\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}}\leq C\left\{\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c_{1}(t-\tau)}\|F_{\infty}\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}}^{2}d\tau\right\}^{\frac{1}{2}}

for
t\in[0,T]
and

F_{\infty}\in L^{2}(0,T;H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s})

with a positive constant
C
depending on
T
.

(iii)

It holds that
r_{H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}}(S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(T))<1
.

(iv)

I-S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(T)
has a bounded inverse
(I-S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(T))^{-1}
on
H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}

and
(I-S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(T))^{-1}
satisfies

\displaystyle\|(I-S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(T))^{-1}u\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}}\leq C\|u\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}}\quad\mbox{for}\quad u\in H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}.

Proof.

Denote

\displaystyle\omega_{1}(T)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\int_{0}^{T}(\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}^{2})dt,

(6.64)

\displaystyle\omega_{2}(t)

\displaystyle=(\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}^{2})-\omega_{1}(T),

\displaystyle z(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}\omega_{2}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau.

Note
z(t+T)=z(t)
, which implies

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}|z(t)|\leq\sup_{\tau\in[0,T]}|z(\tau)|\leq C\delta.

According to Proposition
6.9
with
F_{\infty}=0
, we verify that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)+\kappa(\|\nabla a_{\infty}(t)\|_{H_{\ell}^{s-1}}+\|v_{\infty}(t)\|_{H_{\ell}^{s}})

\displaystyle\leq C(\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{H^{s}}^{2})\|u_{\infty}\|_{H_{\ell}^{s}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\omega_{2}(t)\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)+C\omega_{1}(T)\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t).

By Lemma
4.8
, we see that there exists a positive constant
\kappa_{3}
such that

\displaystyle\kappa(\|\nabla a_{\infty}(t)\|_{H_{\ell}^{s-1}}+\|v_{\infty}(t)\|_{H_{\ell}^{s}})\geq\kappa_{3}\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t).

In terms of (
6.64
) and the above inequality, we achieve that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)+\kappa_{3}\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)&\leq C\omega_{2}(t)\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)+C\omega_{1}(T)\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t).\end{aligned}

Choosing
\delta\leq\frac{\kappa_{3}}{2C}
, we have
\omega_{1}(T)\leq\delta\leq\frac{\kappa_{3}}{2C}
. Then it holds

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)+\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{3}\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)\leq C\omega_{2}(t)\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t).

We thus obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Big(e^{\frac{\kappa_{3}}{2}t}e^{-Cz(t)}\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)\Big)\leq 0.

Integrating it with time yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](t)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](0)e^{-\frac{\kappa_{3}}{2}t}e^{Cz(t)}

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{\kappa_{3}}{2}t+C\delta}\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](0)

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\frac{\kappa_{3}}{4}t}\mathcal{E}_{\ell}^{s}[u_{\infty}](0).

Consequently, choosing
c_{1}=\frac{\kappa_{3}}{4}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|S_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(t)u_{0\infty}\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}}\leq Ce^{-c_{1}t}\|u_{0\infty}\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}}^{s}.

This proves (i). The assertion (ii) is proved similarly, and we omit the proof.

As for (iii), since
\tilde{u}={}^{\top}(\tilde{a},\tilde{v})\in C_{per}(\mathbb{R};H^{s})
, it follows from (i) that, for each
j\in N
,

\|(S_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(T))^{j}u\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}}=\|S_{(\infty),\tilde{u}}(jT)u\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}^{s}}\leq Ce^{-c_{1}jT}\|u\|_{H_{(\infty),\ell}}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\|(S_{(\infty),\tilde{u}}(T))^{j}\|\leq Ce^{-c_{1}jT}.

We thus obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{j\rightarrow{\infty}}\|(S_{(\infty),\tilde{u}}(T))^{j}\|^{\frac{1}{j}}\leq\lim_{j\rightarrow{\infty}}C^{\frac{1}{j}}e^{-c_{1}T}=e^{-c_{1}T}<1.

This show (iii). The assertion (iv) is an immediate consequence of (iii).

\hfill\square

Applying Proposition
6.10
, we easily obtain the following estimate for a solution
u_{\infty}
of
(
4
) satisfying
u_{\infty}(0)=u_{\infty}(T)
.

Proposition 6.11
.

Let
d\geq 3
and
s
be a nonnegative integer satisfying
s\geq[\frac{d}{2}]+2
.
Assume that

\displaystyle F_{\infty}={}^{\top}(F_{\infty}^{0},F_{\infty}^{1})\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{s}_{(\infty),d-1})\cap C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s-1}_{(\infty),d-1}).

Assume also that
\tilde{u}={}^{\top}(\tilde{a},\tilde{v})
satisfies (
6.55
). Then there exists a positive constant
\delta
such that the following assertion holds true if

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}\tilde{\phi}\|_{C([0,T];H^{s-1})}+\|\nabla\tilde{\phi}\|_{C([0,T];H^{s-1})}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{C([0,T];H^{s})}\leq\delta,

The function

\displaystyle u_{\infty}(t):=S_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(t)(I-S_{{\infty},\tilde{u}}(T))^{-1}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(T)[F_{\infty}]+{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,\tilde{u}}(t)[F_{\infty}]

(6.65)

is a solution of (
4
) in
{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}^{s}_{(\infty),d-1}(0,T)

satisfying
u_{\infty}(0)=u_{\infty}(T)
and the estimate

\displaystyle\|u_{\infty}\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}^{s}_{(\infty),d-1}(0,T)}\leq C\|F_{\infty}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s}_{(\infty),d-1}\times H^{s-1}_{(\infty),d-1})}.

7
Proof of Theorem
3.1

In this section we give a proof of Theorem
3.1
.

We first establish the estimates for the nonlinear and inhomogeneous terms

F_{1}(u,g)
and
F_{\infty}(u,g)
:

\displaystyle F_{1}(u,g)

\displaystyle=P_{1}\begin{pmatrix}-v\cdot\nabla a\\
-(v\cdot\nabla v+g_{3}(\phi)\nabla a)+g\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}F_{1}^{1}(u)\\
{F}_{1}^{2}(u,g)\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle F_{\infty}(u,g)

\displaystyle=P_{\infty}\begin{pmatrix}-v\cdot\nabla a_{1}\\
-(v\cdot\nabla v_{1}+g_{3}(\phi)\nabla a_{1})+g\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}F_{\infty}^{1}(u)\\
F_{\infty}^{2}(u,g)\end{pmatrix},

where
u={}^{\top}(a,v)
is a function given by
u_{1}={}^{\top}(a_{1},v_{1})
and
u_{\infty}={}^{\top}(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})
through the relation

\displaystyle a=a_{1}+a_{\infty},\ \ v=v_{1}+v_{\infty}.

We first state the estimates for
F_{1}(u,g)
and
F_{\infty}(u,g)
. We note that

g_{3}(\phi)=P^{\prime}(1+\phi)-P^{\prime}(1)=P^{\prime}(e^{a})-1

and we can rewrite the term
g_{3}(\phi)\nabla a_{1}
as

g_{3}(\phi)\nabla a=(1+g_{2}(\phi)\phi)\nabla(P^{(2)}(\phi)(\phi)^{2}),

where

g^{(2)}(\phi)=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}h(1+\theta\phi)d\theta,\ \ h(t)=\frac{1}{t}

and

P^{(2)}(\phi)=\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}P^{\prime\prime}(1+\theta\phi)d\theta

for
\phi=e^{a}-1
.
For the estimates of the low frequency part, we recall that

\Gamma[{F}_{1}](t):=S_{1}(t){\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(T)(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}F^{1}_{1}\\
{F}^{2}_{1}\end{pmatrix}+{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(t)\begin{pmatrix}F^{1}_{1}\\
{F}_{1}^{2}\end{pmatrix}

We first show the estimate of
\|\Gamma[{F}_{1}(u,g)]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}(0,T)}
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
u_{1}={}^{\top}(a_{1},v_{1})
and
u_{\infty}={}^{\top}(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})
satisfy

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|u_{1}(t)\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|u_{\infty}(t)\|_{H^{s}_{d-1}}\leq\frac{1}{2},

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma[{F}_{1}(u,g)]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}^{(1)}(0,T)}\leq C\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}^{2}+C\Bigl(1+\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}\Bigr)[g]_{s}

uniformly for
u_{1}
and
u_{\infty}
.

Proof.

For
u^{(j)}={}^{\top}(a^{(j)},v^{(j)})
with

\phi^{(j)}=e^{a^{(j)}}-1

(j=1,\infty)
, we set

\displaystyle G_{1}(u^{(1)},u^{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-P_{1}(v^{(1)}\cdot\nabla a^{(2)}),

\displaystyle G_{2}(u^{(1)},u^{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-P_{1}(v^{(1)}\cdot\nabla v^{(2)}),

\displaystyle G_{3}(\phi,u^{(1)},u^{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-P_{1}(\nabla(P^{(1)}(\phi)\phi^{(1)}\phi^{(2)}),

\displaystyle G_{4}(\phi,u^{(1)},u^{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-P_{1}((g^{(2)}(\phi))\nabla(P^{(1)}(\phi)\phi^{(1)}\phi^{(2)}),

\displaystyle H_{k}(u^{(1)},u^{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{k}(u^{(1)},u^{(2)})+G_{k}(u^{(2)},u^{(1)}),\ \ (k=1,2),

\displaystyle H_{k}(\phi,u^{(1)},u^{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{k}(\phi,u^{(1)},u^{(2)})+G_{k}(\phi,u^{(2)},u^{(1)})\ \ (k=3,4).

Then,
\Gamma[\tilde{F}_{1}(u,g)]
is written as

\displaystyle\Gamma[{F}_{1}(u,g)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{2}\left(\Gamma[G_{k}(u_{1},u_{1})]+\Gamma[H_{k}(u_{1},u_{\infty})]+\Gamma[G_{k}(u_{\infty},u_{\infty})]\right)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=3}^{4}(\Gamma[G_{k}(\phi,u_{1},u_{1})]+\Gamma[H_{k}(\phi,u_{1},u_{\infty})]+\Gamma[G_{k}(\phi,u_{\infty},u_{\infty})])

\displaystyle+\Gamma\left[g\right].

Applying
(\ref{propuiestimate1})
to
\Gamma[G_{1}(u_{1},u_{1})]
, and
\Gamma[G_{2}(u_{1},u_{1})]
we have

\|\Gamma[G_{1}(u_{1},u_{1})]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(0,T)}+\Gamma[G_{2}(u_{1},u_{1})]\leq C\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}^{2}.

As for
\Gamma[G_{3}(\phi,u_{1},u_{1})]
, we apply
(\ref{propuiestimate2})
with
F^{(1)}_{1}=P^{(1)}(\phi)\phi_{1}^{2}

(|\alpha|=1)
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\Gamma[G_{3}(u_{1},u_{1})]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(0,T)}\leq C\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}^{2}.

By
(\ref{propuiestimate1})
, we have

\displaystyle\|\sum_{k=1}^{2}\Gamma[G_{k}(u_{\infty},u_{\infty})]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(0,T)}\leq C\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}^{2},

\displaystyle\|\Gamma[G_{3}(\phi,u_{\infty},u_{\infty})]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(0,T)}\leq C\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}^{2}.

By
(\ref{propuiestimate1})
, we also have

\displaystyle\|\sum_{k=1}^{2}\Gamma[G_{k}(u_{1},u_{\infty})]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(0,T)}\leq C\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}^{2},

\displaystyle\|\Gamma[G_{3}(\phi,u_{1},u_{\infty})]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(0,T)}\leq C\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}^{2}.

Concerning
\Gamma[g]
, we see from
(\ref{propuiestimate1})
that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma[g]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}_{(1)}(0,T)}\leq C[g]_{s}.

Therefore, we find that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma[{F}_{1}(u,g)]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}^{(1)}(0,T)}\leq C\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}^{2}+C[g]_{s}.

This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

We next show the estimates for the nonlinear and inhomogeneous terms of the high frequency part.

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
u_{1}={}^{\top}(a_{1},v_{1})
and
u_{\infty}={}^{\top}(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})
satisfy

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|u_{1}(t)\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|u_{\infty}(t)\|_{H^{s}_{d-1}}\leq\frac{1}{2}.

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\|F_{\infty}(u,g)\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s}_{d-1}\times H^{s-1}_{d-1})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}^{2}+C[g]_{s}

uniformly for
u_{1}
and
u_{\infty}
.

Proof.
By a straightforward application of Lemma
2.1
, Lemma
2.3
, Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.8
, we thus arrive at

\displaystyle\|F_{\infty}^{1}(u)\|_{H^{s}_{d-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(1+|x|)^{d-1}\nabla a_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\times(\|(1+|x|)^{d-1}\nabla v_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla v_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|v_{\infty}\|_{H^{s}_{d-1}}),

\displaystyle\|{F}^{2}_{\infty}(u,g))\|_{H^{s-1}_{d-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\Bigl\{\Bigl(\sum_{j=0}^{1}\|(1+|x|)^{d-2+j}\nabla^{j}a_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|a_{\infty}\|_{H^{s}_{d-1}}\Bigr)\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|(1+|x|)^{j-1}\nabla^{j}a_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\|a_{\infty}\|_{H^{s}_{d-1}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\quad+\|(1+|x|)^{d-1}\nabla v_{1}\|_{L^{\infty}}(\|v_{\infty}\|_{H^{s}}+\|v_{1}\|_{L^{2}})+\|g\|_{H^{s}_{d-1}}.

Integrating these inequalities on
(0,T)
, we obtain the desired estimate.
This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

We next estimate
F_{1}(u^{(1)},g)-F_{1}(u^{(2)},g)
.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
u^{(k)}_{1}={}^{\top}(a^{(k)}_{1},v^{(k)}_{1})
and
u^{(k)}_{\infty}={}^{\top}(a^{(k)}_{\infty},v^{(k)}_{\infty})
satisfy

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|u^{(k)}_{1}(t)\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|u^{(k)}_{\infty}(t)\|_{H^{s}_{n-1}}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\phi^{(k)}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{1}{2},

where
\phi^{(k)}=\phi^{(k)}_{1}+\phi^{(k)}_{\infty}

(k=1,2)
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma[{F}_{1}(u^{(1)},g)-{F}_{1}(u^{(2)},g)]\|_{{\mbox{\sy Z}\,}^{(1)}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{k=1}^{2}\|\{u^{(k)}_{1},u^{(k)}_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}\|\{u^{(1)}_{1}-u^{(2)}_{1},u^{(1)}_{\infty}-u^{(2)}_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s-1}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\quad+C[g]_{s}\|\{u^{(1)}_{1}-u^{(2)}_{1},u^{(1)}_{\infty}-u^{(2)}_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s-1}(0,T)}

uniformly for
u^{(k)}_{1}
and
u^{(k)}_{\infty}
.

Proposition
7.3
can be proved in a similar manner to the proof of Proposition
7.1
; and
we omit the proof.

We next estimate
F_{\infty}(u^{(1)},g)-F_{\infty}(u^{(2)},g)
.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
u^{(k)}_{1}={}^{\top}(a^{(k)}_{1},v^{(k)}_{1})
and
u^{(k)}_{\infty}={}^{\top}(a^{(k)}_{\infty},v^{(k)}_{\infty})
satisfy

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|u^{(k)}_{1}(t)\|_{{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|u^{(k)}_{\infty}(t)\|_{H^{s}_{-1}}+\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\phi^{(k)}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{1}{2},

where
\phi^{(k)}=\phi^{(k)}_{1}+\phi^{(k)}_{\infty}

(k=1,2)
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle\|F_{\infty}(u^{(1)},g)-F_{\infty}(u^{(2)},g)]\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{s-1}_{d-1}\times H^{s-2}_{d-1})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{k=1}^{2}\|\{u^{(k)}_{1},u^{(k)}_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}\|\{u^{(1)}_{1}-u^{(2)}_{1},u^{(1)}_{\infty}-u^{(2)}_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s-1}(0,T)}

\displaystyle\quad+C[g]_{s}\|\{u^{(1)}_{1}-u^{(2)}_{1},u^{(1)}_{\infty}-u^{(2)}_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s-1}(0,T)}

uniformly for
u^{(k)}_{1}
and
u^{(k)}_{\infty}
.

Proposition
7.4
directly follows from Lemmas
2.1
–
2.3
, Lemma
4.3
, Lemma
4.8
and Lemma
4.9
in a similar manner to the proof of Proposition
7.2
.

We next show the following estimate which will be used in the proof of Proposition
7.5
.

To prove Theorem
3.1
,
we next show the existence of a solution
\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}

of (
4
) on
[0,T]

satisfying
u_{1}(0)=u_{1}(T)
and
u_{\infty}(0)=u_{\infty}(T)
by an iteration argument.

For
N=0,

we define
u_{1}^{(0)}={}^{\top}(a_{1}^{(0)},v_{1}^{(0)})

and
u_{\infty}^{(0)}={}^{\top}(a_{\infty}^{(0)},v_{\infty}^{(0)})

by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}u_{1}^{(0)}(t)&=S_{1}(t){\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(T)[(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}\mathbb{G}_{1}]+{{\mbox{\sy S}\,}}_{1}(t)[\mathbb{G}_{1}],\\
u_{\infty}^{(0)}(t)&=S_{\infty,0}(t)(I-S_{{\infty},0}(T))^{-1}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,0}(T)[\mathbb{G}_{\infty}]+{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,0}(t)[\mathbb{G}_{\infty}],\end{array}\right.

where
t\in[0,T]
,

\mathbb{G}={}^{\top}(0,g(x,t))
,
\mathbb{G}_{1}=P_{1}\mathbb{G}

,
\mathbb{G}_{\infty}=P_{\infty}\mathbb{G}
,
a^{(0)}=a^{(0)}_{1}+a^{(0)}_{\infty}
and
v^{(0)}=v^{(0)}_{1}+v^{(0)}_{\infty}
.
Note that
u^{(0)}_{1}(0)=u^{(0)}_{1}(T)
and
u^{(0)}_{\infty}(0)=u^{(0)}_{\infty}(T)
.

For
N\geq 1
,
we define

u_{1}^{(N)}={}^{\top}(a_{1}^{(N)},v_{1}^{(N)})

and
u_{\infty}^{(N)}={}^{\top}(a_{\infty}^{(N)},v_{\infty}^{(N)})
, inductively, by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll|}u_{1}^{(N)}(t)&=S_{1}(t){\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{1}(T)[(I-S_{1}(T))^{-1}F_{1}(u^{(N-1)},g)]+{{\mbox{\sy S}\,}}_{1}(t)[F_{1}(u^{(N-1)},g)],\\
u_{\infty}^{(N)}(t)&=S_{\infty,u^{(N-1)}}(t)(I-S_{{\infty},u^{(N-1)}}(T))^{-1}{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,u^{(N-1)}}(T)[F_{\infty}(u^{(N-1)},g)]\\
&\quad+{\mbox{\sy S}\,}_{\infty,u^{(N-1)}}(t)[F_{\infty}(u^{(N-1)},g)],\end{array}\right.

where
t\in[0,T]
,
u^{(N-1)}=u_{1}^{(N-1)}+u_{\infty}^{(N-1)}
,
u^{(N-1)}_{1}={}^{\top}(a^{(N-1)}_{1},v^{(N-1)}_{1})
,
a^{(N)}=a^{(N)}_{1}+a^{(N)}_{\infty}
and
v^{(N)}=v^{(N)}_{1}+v^{(N)}_{\infty}
.
Note that
u^{(N)}_{1}(0)=u^{(N)}_{1}(T)
and
u^{(0)}_{\infty}(0)=u^{(0)}_{\infty}(T)
.

Proposition 7.5
.

There exists a constant
\delta_{1}>0
such that
if
[g]_{s}\leq\delta_{1}
,
then there holds the estimates

\|\{u^{(N)}_{1},u^{(N)}_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}\leq C_{1}[g]_{s}

[formula]

for all
N\geq 0
, and

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\lx@intercol\lx@add@cssclass{ltx_eqn_lefteqn}$\displaystyle\|\{u^{(N+1)}_{1}-u^{(N)}_{1},u^{(N+1)}_{\infty}-u^{(N)}_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}$\mbox{}\hfil\lx@intercol\\[8.61108pt]
&\leq&C_{1}[g]_{s}\|\{u^{(N)}_{1}-u^{(N-1)}_{1},u^{(N)}_{\infty}-u^{(N-1)}_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{s}(0,T)}\end{array}

[formula]

for
N\geq 1
.
Here
C_{1}
is a constant independent of
g
and
N
.

Proof.

If
[g]_{s}\leq\delta_{1}
for sufficiently small
\delta_{1}
, the estimate (i) and (ii) follows from Propositions
5.1
,
6.11
,
7.1
,
7.2
.

\hfill\square

Before going further, we introduce a notation. We denote by
B_{X^{k}(a,b)}(r)

the closed unit ball of
X^{k}(a,b)
centered at
0
with radius
r
, i.e.,

B_{X^{k}(a,b)}(r)=\left\{\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\in X^{k}(a,b);\|\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\|_{X^{k}(a,b)}\leq r\right\}.

Proposition 7.6
.

There exists a constant
\delta_{2}>0
such that
if
[g]_{s}\leq\delta_{2}
,
then the system (
4
) has a unique solution
\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}

on
[0,T]
in
B_{X^{s}(0,T)}(C_{1}[g]_{s})
satisfying
u_{1}(0)=u_{1}(T)
and
u_{\infty}(0)=u_{\infty}(T)
.
The uniqueness of solutions of (
4
) on
[0,T]

satisfying
u_{1}(0)=u_{1}(T)
and
u_{\infty}(0)=u_{\infty}(T)

holds in
B_{X^{s}(0,T)}(C_{1}\delta_{2})
.

Proof.

Let
\delta_{2}=\min\{\delta_{1},\frac{1}{2C_{1}}\}

with
\delta_{1}
given in Propositions
7.5
.
By Propositions
7.5
,
we see that
if
[g]_{s}\leq\delta_{2}
,
then
u^{(N)}_{1}={}^{\top}(a_{1}^{(N)},v_{1}^{(N)})
and
u^{(N)}_{\infty}={}^{\top}(a_{\infty}^{(N)},v_{\infty}^{(N)})

converge to
u_{1}={}^{\top}(\phi_{1},m_{1})
and
u_{\infty}={}^{\top}(a_{\infty},v_{\infty})
, respectively,
in the sense

\{u^{(N)}_{1},u_{\infty}^{(N)}\}\ \rightarrow\ \{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}\ \mbox{\rm in }\ X^{s}(0,T).

It is not difficult to see that
\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}
is a solution of (
4
)
satisfying
u_{1}(0)=u_{1}(T)
and
u_{\infty}(0)=u_{\infty}(T)
. This completes the proof.

\hfill\square

We can now construct a time periodic solution of (
4.1
)-(
4.2
) in the same argument as that in
[
10
]
.
As in
[
10
]
, based on the estimates in sections 6-8, one can show the following proposition on the unique existence of solutions of the
initial value problem.

Proposition 7.7
.

Let
h\in\mathbb{R}
and let
u_{0}=u_{01}+u_{0\infty}

with
u_{01}\in{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}}_{(1)}\times{{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}}_{(1)}
and
u_{0\infty}\in H_{(\infty),d-1}^{s}
.
Then there exist constants
\delta_{4}>0
and
C_{3}>0
such that
if

M(U_{01},U_{0\infty},g):=\|u_{01}\|_{{\mbox{\sy X}\,}^{(1)}\times{\mbox{\sy Y}\,}^{(1)}}+\|u_{0\infty}\|_{H^{s}_{(\infty),d-1}}+[g]_{s}\leq\delta_{4},

there exists a solution
\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}

of the initial value problem for (
4.1
)-(
4.2
)
on
[h,h+T]
in
B_{X^{s}(h,h+T)}(C_{3}M(u_{01},u_{0\infty},g))

satisfying the initial condition
u_{j}|_{t=h}=u_{0j}

(j=0,\infty)
.
The uniqueness for this initial value problem holds in
B_{X^{s}(h,h+T)}(C_{3}\delta_{4})
.

By using Proposition
7.7
, one can extend
\{u_{1},u_{\infty}\}
periodically on

\mathbb{R}
as a time periodic solution of (
4.1
)-(
4.2
). Since the argument for extension is the same as that given in
[
10
]
,
we here omit the details. Consequently, we obtain Theorem
3.1
. This completes the proof.

8
Proof of Theorem
3.3
.

The local existence theorem is verified by a similar iteration argument to that in Proposition
6.4
based on Lemma
6.1
. Indeed, we consider the following system;

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}{\psi}^{(0)}+\mbox{\rm div}\,w^{(0)}+v_{per}\cdot\nabla{\psi}^{(0)}+w^{(0)}\cdot\nabla a_{per}=0,\\
\partial_{t}{w}^{(0)}+w^{(0)}+\nabla\psi^{(0)}+v_{per}\cdot\nabla{w}^{(0)}+{w}^{(0)}\cdot\nabla v_{per}=0,\end{array}\right.

and for
1\leq N

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\partial_{t}{\psi}^{(N)}+\mbox{\rm div}\,w^{(N)}+v_{per}\cdot\nabla{\psi}^{(N)}+w^{(N)}\cdot\nabla a_{per}+w^{(N-1)}\cdot\nabla\psi^{(N)}=0,\\
\partial_{t}{w}^{(N)}+w^{(N)}+\nabla\psi^{(N)}+v_{per}\cdot\nabla{w}^{(N)}+{w}^{(N)}\cdot\nabla v_{per}\\
\quad+g_{4}(a_{per},\psi^{(N)})\nabla\psi^{(N)}+g_{5}(a_{per},\psi^{(N)})\nabla a_{per}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+w^{(N-1)}\cdot\nabla{w}^{(N)}=0.\end{array}\right.

Then the local existence of a solution to (
8
) is directly obtained by Lemma
6.1
. A similar iteration argument to that in Proposition
6.4
based on Lemma
6.1
derives a fixed point of (
8
),
i.e., a solution to (
3
) in
C([0,T^{\prime}];H^{s})
. We note that by
\eqref{stability}_{1}

\partial_{t}\psi\in C([0,T];H^{s-1})
and

\|{}^{\top}(\psi,w)\|_{C([0,T];H^{s})}+\|\partial_{t}\psi\|_{C([0,T];H^{s-1})}\leq C\|{}^{\top}(\psi_{0},w_{0})\|_{H^{s}},

where
T>0
is a fixed time and
C
depends on
T
.

We note that Remark
3.2
and Lemmas
2.1
imply that if

\|u\|_{C([0,T];H^{s})}\leq\delta,

then

\|g_{j}(a_{per},\psi)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|g_{j}(a_{per},\psi)\|_{H^{s}}\leq C_{\delta}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}

for
j=4,5

Hence we show the energy estimate
\eqref{energy-stability}_{2}
which implies
\eqref{energy-stability}_{1}
and
\eqref{energy-stability}_{3}
.
To prove
\eqref{energy-stability}_{2}
, we divide it into three steps.

Lemma 8.1
.

(Step 1:
0
-th order energy inequality)
We assume that there exists a solution
u={}^{\top}(\psi,w)
to (
3
) satisfying

\|u\|_{C([0,T];H^{s})}+\|\partial_{t}\psi\|_{C([0,T];H^{s-1})}\leq\delta_{1}\leq 1.

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\delta_{1}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.8)

Proof.

We assume that

\|u\|_{C([0,T];H^{s})}+\|\partial_{t}\psi\|_{C([0,T];H^{s-1})}\leq\delta_{1}\leq 1.

Multiplying (
3
) by
\psi
and
w
respectively, and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by parts then adding them together, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(\mbox{\rm div}\,w,\psi)

\displaystyle=-(v_{per}\cdot\nabla\psi,\psi)-(w\cdot\nabla a_{per},\psi)+(f^{1}(u),\psi)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{3}\tilde{I}_{k}.

By the Hardy inequality and Sobolev inequality, we obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{I}_{1}&\leq C\|v_{per}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d-2}}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}},\\
\tilde{I}_{2}&\leq C\|\nabla a_{per}\|_{L^{\infty}_{d-1}}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\\
\tilde{I}_{3}&\leq C\|\psi\|_{L^{\infty}}\|w\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\delta_{1}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.9)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(\nabla\psi,w)+\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{5}\tilde{J}_{k},

(8.10)

where

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{J}_{1}&=-(v_{per}\cdot\nabla w,w),\\
\tilde{J}_{2}&=-(w\cdot\nabla v_{per},w),\\
\tilde{J}_{3}&=-(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla\psi,w),\\
\tilde{J}_{4}&=-(g_{5}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla a_{per},w),\\
\tilde{J}_{5}&=-(w\cdot\nabla w,w).\end{aligned}

(8.11)

The Hardy inequality and Sobolev inequality yield similarly that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{5}\tilde{J}_{k}\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\delta_{1}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Consequently, we get that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\delta_{1}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(8.12)

∎

Lemma 8.2
.

(Step 2: Higher order energy inequality)
We assume that there exists a solution
u={}^{\top}(\psi,w)
to (
3
) satisfying

\|u\|_{C([0,T];H^{s})}+\|\partial_{t}\psi\|_{C([0,T];H^{s-1})}\leq\delta_{1}\leq 1.

Then there holds that for
1\leq|\alpha|\leq s

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mbox{\rm div}\,w)\\
&\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\delta_{0}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2})\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2},\\
&\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mbox{\rm div}\,w)+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi|^{2}dx\\
&\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C(\delta_{0}+\delta_{1})\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.13)

Proof.

For
1\leq|\alpha|\leq s
, applying
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}
to (
3
) and multiplying by
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}u
, and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, then adding them together, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mbox{\rm div}\,w)+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{3}\tilde{I}_{\alpha,k},\\
&\frac{1}{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mbox{\rm div}\,w)+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{5}\tilde{J}_{\alpha,k},\\
\end{aligned}

(8.14)

where

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{I}_{\alpha,1}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(v_{per}\cdot\nabla\psi),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi),\\
\tilde{I}_{\alpha,2}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(w\cdot\nabla a_{per}),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi),\\
\tilde{I}_{\alpha,3}&=(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f^{1}(u),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi),\end{aligned}

(8.15)

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{J}_{\alpha,1}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(v_{per}\cdot\nabla w)),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w),\\
\tilde{J}_{\alpha,2}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(w\cdot\nabla v_{per}),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w),\\
\tilde{J}_{\alpha,3}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla\psi),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w),\\
\tilde{J}_{\alpha,4}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(g_{5}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla a_{per}),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w),\\
\tilde{J}_{\alpha,5}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(w\cdot\nabla w),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w).\end{aligned}

(8.16)

We see from the Sobolev inequality with Lemma
2.3
that

\displaystyle\tilde{I}_{\alpha,1}+\tilde{I}_{\alpha,3}\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C(\delta_{0}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2})\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}.

On
\tilde{I}_{\alpha,2}
, since

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{I}_{\alpha,2}&=(w\cdot\nabla\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a_{per},\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi)\\
&\quad+(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(w\cdot\nabla a_{per})-w\cdot\nabla\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a_{per},\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi),\end{aligned}

(8.17)

the Sobolev inequality with Lemma
2.3
verifies that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{I}_{\alpha,2}&\leq C\|w\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\partial_{x}^{\alpha}a_{per}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\nabla a_{per}\|_{H^{s-1}}\|w\|_{H^{s}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.18)

Here we used that
\nabla a_{per}\in H^{s}
. On
\tilde{J}_{\alpha,k}

(k=1,\cdots,5)
, the Sobolev inequality with Lemma
2.3
similarly yield that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{J}_{\alpha,1}+\tilde{J}_{\alpha,2}+\tilde{J}_{\alpha,5}&\leq\epsilon\|w_{H^{s}}\|^{2}+C(\delta_{0}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2})\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2},\\
\tilde{J}_{\alpha,4}&\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.19)

On
\tilde{J}_{\alpha,3}
, set

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{J}_{\alpha,3}&=-(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w)\\
&\quad-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla\psi)-g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w)\\
&:=\tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,1}+\tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,2}.\end{aligned}

(8.20)

The integration by parts with substituting
\eqref{stability}_{1}
derive that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,1}&=(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mbox{\rm div}\,w)\\
&\quad+(\nabla g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w)\\
&=-(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{t}\psi)\\
&\quad-(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(v_{per}\cdot\nabla\psi))\\
&\quad-(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(w\cdot\nabla a_{per}))\\
&\quad-(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(w\cdot\nabla\psi))\\
&\quad+(\nabla g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}w)\\
&:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{5}\tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,1,\ell}.\end{aligned}

(8.21)

\tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,1,1}
is estimated as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,1,1}&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi|^{2}dx\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial_{t}g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi|^{2}dx\\
&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi|^{2}dx\\
&\quad+C(\|\partial_{t}\phi_{per}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\partial_{t}\psi\|_{L^{\infty}})\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi|^{2}dx\\
&\quad+C(\delta_{0}+\delta_{1})\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.22)

Here we used that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\partial_{t}\phi_{per}\|_{H^{s-1}}\leq C\delta_{0}
and
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\partial_{t}\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}\leq\delta_{1}
.

\tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,1,2}
is estimated by the Sobolev inequality with Lemma
2.3
as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,1,2}&\leq-(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,v_{per}\cdot\nabla\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi)\\
&\quad-(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(v_{per}\cdot\nabla\psi)-v_{per}\cdot\nabla\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi)\\
&\leq C\|g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}(\|\nabla v_{per}\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}v_{per}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{\infty}})\\
&\quad+C\|\mbox{\rm div}\,(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)v_{per})\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\leq C\|\nabla\psi_{per}\|_{H^{s-1}}\|\nabla v_{per}\|_{H^{s-1}}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}\\
&\leq C\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.23)

It follows a similar argument as that in (
8.23
) that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,1,3}+\tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,1,4}\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.24)

The Sobolev inequality with Lemma
2.3
again verifies that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,1,5}+\tilde{J}_{\alpha,3,2}\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.25)

Therefore we obtain that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{J}_{\alpha,3}&\leq-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi|^{2}dx\\
&\quad+\epsilon\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.26)

\tilde{J}_{\alpha,4}
can be estimated directly by the Sobolev inequality with Lemma
2.3
as

\displaystyle\tilde{J}_{\alpha,4}\leq\epsilon\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+C\delta_{0}^{2}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.

Combining the above estimates together, we can prove (
8.13
).
∎

Lemma 8.3
.

(Step 3: Deriving the dissipation term of density)
We assume that there exists a solution
u={}^{\top}(\psi,w)
to (
3
) satisfying

\displaystyle\|u\|_{C([0,T];H^{s})}\leq\delta_{1}\leq 1.

Then there holds that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}w,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi)+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\\
&\quad\leq C_{1}(\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}+\delta_{0}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2})\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}+C(\delta_{0}+\delta_{1})\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.27)

Proof.

We consider that for
1\leq|\alpha|\leq s
, applying
\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}
to
\eqref{stability}_{2}
and multiplying by
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi
, and integrating over
\mathbb{R}^{3}
to get

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}w,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi)+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{8}\tilde{G}_{\alpha,k},

(8.28)

where

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \tilde{G}_{\alpha,1}&=(\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}w,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi),\\
\tilde{G}_{\alpha,2}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}w,\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi),\\
\tilde{G}_{\alpha,3}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}(v_{per}\cdot\nabla w),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi),\\
\tilde{G}_{\alpha,4}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}(w\cdot\nabla v_{per}),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi),\\
\tilde{G}_{\alpha,5}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}(g_{4}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla\psi),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi),\\
\tilde{G}_{\alpha,6}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}(g_{5}(a_{per},\psi)\nabla a_{per}),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi),\\
\tilde{G}_{\alpha,7}&=-(\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}(w\cdot\nabla w),\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\psi).\end{aligned}

(8.29)

The Sobolev inequality with Lemma
2.3
directly show that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sum_{k=2}^{8}\tilde{G}_{\alpha,k}\leq\frac{C}{\epsilon_{1}}\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}+C(\delta_{0}+\delta_{1})\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s}}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(8.30)

On
\tilde{G}_{\alpha,1}
, substituting
\eqref{stability}_{1}
to
\partial_{t}\psi
, the Sobolev inequality with Lemma
2.3
verifies that

\displaystyle\tilde{G}_{\alpha,1}\leq C_{1}(\frac{1}{\epsilon_{1}}+\delta_{0}^{2}+\delta_{1}^{2})\|w\|_{H^{s}}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}\|\nabla\psi\|_{H^{s-1}}^{2}.

Combining the above estimates together, we prove (
8.27
).
∎

We take
\epsilon=\frac{1}{4}
,
\epsilon_{1}=\frac{1}{2}
and a large constant
C_{2}
satisfying that

C_{2}>4C_{1}
independent
\delta_{0}
and
\delta_{1}
.
When we take summation on
\alpha
and
add (
8.8
),
C_{2}\times\eqref{energy-higer-order-stability}
and (
8.27
) with taking small
\delta_{0}
and
\delta_{1}
, we get
\eqref{energy-stability}_{2}
. This completes the proof.
